It is wrong by **OMISSION** — missing every odd multiple of π, since θ=π satisfies the equation (`tan 0 = 0 = cot(−π/2)`) while the π/2 branch it excludes is inadmissible anyway. Complete set `θ = rπ` matches no option ⇒ **D**. My read-through checked that π/2 was inadmissible **and stopped there**. A `solution_audit` cannot reach this class by construction, which is exactly why 0075's `METHOD_STRENGTH` refuses to let a read-through excuse a blind pass.
  - **THE BLIND PASS ALSO RE-PROVED THE MORNING'S WORK.** Three separate agents, each seeing only stem+options, independently returned **Q941→D**, **Q2017→C** and reproduced the **NDA 2023-I two-triangle ambiguity** from scratch. Those three corrections had rested on my own derivation alone; they no longer do. **Independent confirmation is worth as much as the find** — it is the difference between "I checked it" and "it was checked".
  - **THREE DEFECTS, SOURCE-ADJUDICATED, AND THEY HAD THREE DIFFERENT OWNERS** (booklets located via `scripts/practice/config.ts`, all with usable text layers): **Q2666 was OURS** — the ingest dropped "The minimum value of", leaving a stem naming no quantity; printed key (a) already matched, stem restored. **Q2526 is the SOURCE's** — the booklet genuinely prints both `[-1,∞)` and `(-1,∞)`, and BOTH are true (strict monotonicity does not require f′>0 at an isolated endpoint), printed key (d) retained with a note saying plainly that a student answering (a) is not wrong. **Q761 is the SOURCE's** — it prints `a=1/3` and `c=4/12`, the same number, both distractors, printed key (d) matches. **The lesson: a defect report is a HYPOTHESIS ABOUT WHOSE DEFECT IT IS, and only the source settles it** — the same rule that fired that morning when I called the NDA 2023-I question defective before checking and was rightly challenged.
  - **ALL FOUR DEFECTS WERE `practice` ROWS; NOT ONE WAS A PYQ.** They came from the vision-ingested practice pipeline ([[practice-pdf-vision-ingestion]]). That matters for what to review next: papers hold **1,843 practice questions vs 1,211 PYQ**, and the 2026-07-25 `audit:keys` sweep sanitised only the STRUCTURAL class (solution-contradicts-key · duplicate options · not-exactly-one-correct). **Q1217 is none of those** — coherent solution, distinct options, exactly one flagged correct, key still wrong. That *stealth* class has never been swept, and this pass found it at ~1 per 180. Prioritise practice-heavy papers; the estimate rests on one sample, so measure two more before committing to a bank-wide sweep.
  - **A COLLISION IN MY OWN TOOL, same family as the dedupe lesson written hours earlier.** `reviews:paper` used `paper:<id>` as the run label **regardless of method**, so the blind rows collided with the morning's `solution_audit` rows on `(question_id, run_label, hash)` and **118 confirmations were silently discarded** — only Q1217 survived, because flipping its key changed the hash. The tell was again a count that had no business being what it was ("0 recorded | 118 already on record"). Now method-qualified. New tooling: `diff-blind.ts` (prints disagreements as a **work list, never a verdict**) and `triage-dump.ts`, which **refuses to run against a blind dump** — it would otherwise flag all 120 rows `NO_SOLUTION`, and a probe that fires on everything buries its one real hit. Agent derivations are committed under `scripts/reviews/data/blind/` as the auditable artifact.

- **2026-08-11 — Review provenance now EMITS ITSELF: nine pipeline scripts record as they run, `*.errata.json` backfilled (+114), and the two surfaces that were doing the most review work with the least record — printed papers and report triage — got CLIs (migration 0075).** Follows the 0074 ship + backfill below. Table now **952 rows / 943 questions**.
  - **THE QUESTION THAT FOUND THE BIGGEST GAP was the user's: "there are papers in the paper section — how are their reviews handled?"** Answer: they weren't. **50 papers · 3,431 placements · 3,054 distinct questions · ZERO rows** — more than 3× everything the backfill recovered, and it is the review that runs most often (read the solutions before printing). Worse, questions repeat across papers, so the same question was being re-derived on every appearance with no way to know it had already been checked. Same shape for report triage: the recorder was bolted to the web route, so resolving a report from a script — **the common path** — wrote nothing at all. Both now have CLIs (`reviews:paper`, `reviews:resolve`), and the route's private recorder MOVED into the shared `emit.ts` so the two cannot drift.
  - **MIGRATION 0075 adds `solution_audit`, and the reason is that the distinction is LOAD-BEARING, not descriptive.** A pre-print review is usually a read-through of the stored solution; a blind re-derivation is a different animal. Labelling the first as the second would overstate evidence *inside the audit trail* — the failure this table exists to prevent, one level up. What makes it more than a label: **it decides what a later pass may SKIP.** A question confirmed by blind re-derivation is done; one confirmed by a read-through must still be shown to a blind pass, because **a stealth wrong key survives a read-through by construction** (the 2026-06-03 audit's central finding: most of its ~235 flips were solutions that were internally consistent and simply wrong). Proven live on a 60-q paper: after two read-through confirmations a second read-through skipped them (58 left) while a blind pass still showed all 60. `METHOD_STRENGTH` + `classifyForReview` (TDD) carry it; a known defect **outranks a skip**, because before printing "this question has a known defect" is exactly what the reviewer needs.
  - **NOTHING AMBIGUOUS EMITS.** An mcq mismatch gets no row — the script it comes from "never auto-re-keys, that needs a human adjudication", so recording a verdict first would assert an outcome nobody reached. `mh-ssc-10`'s wording repairs get none either: that pass recorded 0 ours-wrong, and a corrective verdict would contradict it. Report triage derives `confirmed` only from `wont-fix` on an answer-affecting category (unambiguous: a claim was rejected, so the answer stands) and otherwise requires an explicit pick.
  - **THREE DEFECTS FOUND BY USING THE TOOLS, NOT READING THEM** — each invisible to the gate. (1) The paper dump omitted `context`, so set-based questions ("Consider the following for the next two items…") read as unanswerable — **it nearly produced a false defect report on the very first run**, and only checking the DB stopped it. (2) **Re-dumping silently destroyed filled-in verdicts** — I did exactly that to my own work; it now refuses without `--force`. (3) `reviews:report` crashed with `Bad Request` the first time its coverage section met real data: **`.in()` with 833 uuids exceeds the PostgREST URL limit — paging a RESULT (1000) and chunking a FILTER (~200) are different limits and only one of them is 1000.**
  - **A REAL FINDING ON A PAPER ABOUT TO BE PRINTED, and the user's push-back is the lesson.** Reviewing 3 questions of an NDA paper surfaced that Q36's solution opened "From Q35" while Q35 was not in the paper. Chasing it showed the underlying **NDA 2023-I Q35/Q36 pair is under-determined**: `sin A = √3/2` admits **A = 120°** as well as 60°, giving a genuine triangle (√3,1,1) that satisfies every printed condition, with perimeter **√3+2 — printed option (b) of Q35** — under which Q36 flips from (c) to (d). **I asserted "the source is defective" BEFORE verifying it, and was right to be challenged**: the documented rule here is that the bank-side is the ~80% default suspect and a stem must be source-verified before the source is blamed. Rendered `Maths_2023_NDA1.pdf` p.13 and read it — our stem is word-for-word faithful, nothing dropped — then confirmed the ambiguity with a **brute-force numeric scan that found both branches without being pointed at a second one**. Keys **retained** (C and C) per the preserve-paper-defects convention: changing a PYQ key on our own derivation would be worse, since (b) is equally defensible and students were marked against the issued key. Solutions rewritten with an honest bracket; Q36 made self-contained. **Verified hash-neutral**: `content_hash` excludes `solution`, so both hashes are byte-identical before and after (`9182ef5c`, `4729aedc`). **Stated limit, recorded in the row itself: no official UPSC key exists in the source folder, so the note claims the QUESTION admits two answers — never that the published key is wrong.** And a limit on the check itself: I had already seen the stored key, so this was NOT a blind derivation; `reviews:paper --method=blind_rederivation` withholds key + solution precisely so a future pass can be.
  - **The shell-escaping trap fired THREE times in one session** (a `node -e` regex mangled to literal newlines, a `python -c` heredoc, an inline verifier) — every one fixed by authoring through the Write tool instead. [[heredoc-backslash-eating]] is not a subagent-only hazard. Verified: ~80 tests across 7 review suites, full gate PASS, 625 prerendered `.html`, backfill re-run writes 0.

- **2026-08-11 — Review provenance BACKFILLED: 833 questions (2.0% of the PUBLIC bank) now carry a record, reconstructed from the 55 committed artifacts — and the run exposed a defect that had silently stored 10 wrong verdicts.** Follows the 0074 ship below. 844 artifact rows → 834 stored rows across 51 runs, all `source='backfilled'`: **813 confirmed · 19 defect_preserved · 2 unverifiable · ZERO corrected**. That last number is the result: across every recorded review, not one "our answer was wrong" finding survived adjudication, so the Decisions-log claim that source keys fail far more often than our answers now stands as data rather than prose.
  - **RESOLUTION WAS 100%, which is why this was worth doing at all.** All 566 ref-only rows resolve via `(source_file, question_number)` — verified unique — and all 278 id-bearing rows point at live questions; 844/844 → 833 distinct, every one PUBLIC. The per-file counts reconcile *exactly* against the Decisions log (mh-ssc-10 gives 279 AGREE / 14 BOOK-WRONG / 0 ours-wrong; NCERT gives 2 book-key errors across ~256 exercise questions), which is the best available evidence the ref mapping is right.
  - **THE DEFECT, and it is the transferable part: the dedupe constraint resolved a genuine disagreement by `readdir` order.** NCERT `Ex 7.8 Q13-22` sit in TWO overlapping agent batches of one cross-check that **disagree on all ten** — `integrals.ex678` says CANT-READ-KEY ("the reprint lists Ex 7.8 answers 1-12 only"), `integrals.ex8910` says AGREE *and transcribes a concrete printed key for every one*, having found them under the old Ex 7.9 numbering exactly as the other batch guessed they might be. Same question, same `run_label`, same hash ⇒ `UNIQUE (question_id, run_label, reviewed_content_hash)` collapsed each pair and kept whichever file the OS listed first — **the weaker verdict, on all ten**. Caught only by re-checking a number I had already called "benign" (844 rows → 834 written, 10 deduped). **A dedupe key silently picks a winner; if the rows it merges can DISAGREE, that is an adjudication being made by file order.** Now `findVerdictConflicts` blocks a conflict *within* one run, while a disagreement *between* runs still lands — that is real history, not a conflict. Adjudicated all ten to `confirmed` (7 keys match verbatim; Q16 distributes to ours, Q17 is term order, Q20 is `4/π−2√2/π = (4−2√2)/π`, each checked by hand): **evidence beats absence of evidence.**
  - **Nothing ambiguous maps automatically.** `OUR-ANSWER-WRONG` has no mechanical mapping — the artifact says our answer was judged wrong, never what followed — and its single instance (NCERT Ex 7.9 Q9) was **overturned**, our (A) right and the printed key wrong. Mapping it would have inverted the record. Same for an mcq-verify row disagreeing with the live key: either the key was fixed later or the question is defective and the official key was deliberately kept, and guessing between them is not available. **13 adjudications live in `scripts/reviews/backfill-overrides.json`**, each citing its Decisions-log date; a stale entry that matches nothing is a BLOCKING error, so the file cannot rot.
  - **Prose is never a source.** Only committed machine-readable artifacts are read. A `*.crosscheck.json` row carries a ref, a verdict and a derivation written by the pass that did the work; a Decisions-log paragraph re-read by an LLM is a plausible reconstruction, and this table exists precisely so a reconstruction and a record stop looking alike. Hence `source='backfilled'` on all 834.
  - **The one genuine compromise, stated in the script:** a backfilled row stamps the question's CURRENT `content_hash`, because the hash *as at review* is unrecoverable — `last_edited_at` is **NULL on all 833** and the ingestion scripts update `questions` without setting it, so it proves nothing. So the fingerprint means "as at backfill": drift after this run is detected normally, drift before it is undetectable by construction. The alternative (a sentinel) would mark all 833 permanently stale and make the report's stale section useless on day one.
  - **`reviews:report` crashed the first time its coverage section met real data** — `.in()` with 833 uuids exceeds the PostgREST URL limit (`Bad Request`). **Paging a RESULT and chunking a FILTER are different limits and only one of them is 1000**; both scripts now chunk `.in()` at 200. Another instance of [[probe-must-reach-the-code]]: the path had only ever run against an empty table. Verified: 19 artifact-mapping unit tests, full gate PASS, 625 prerendered `.html`, re-running the backfill writes 0 (idempotent), stale = 0. Coverage lands at CBSE Class 12 Maths **53.6%**, MH-SB-11 7.8%, MH-HSC-12 7.0%, MH-SB-9 4.9%; everything else reads 0, meaning *not recorded*. See [[question-review-provenance]].

- **2026-08-11 — Review provenance shipped (migration 0074 + `npm run reviews:report`): the bank can now say which questions nobody has checked.** Prompted by the question "we review question solution — do we maintain record of it?". The audit found the answer is *partly, and not queryably*: the Decisions log carries the narrative (Phase 7 of `/solution-cleanup` mandates an entry), `question_reports` covers inbound student reports, and three in-band markers cover slivers — `derived_model`/`derived_at` on 1,093 of 44,220 rows, 533 `pyq_note` provenance clauses, 206 `[Textbook…]` errata brackets. Nothing recorded *this question was reviewed, by this method, with this verdict*.
  - **THE DISTINCTION THAT MADE IT TRACTABLE: provenance of CONTENT was already shipped five times over — provenance of REVIEW never was.** `derived_model` (0040), `source_file`/`pyq_note`, the `[Q# · month · year]` student citation, written-paper slot provenance, sync metadata (0010) all answer *where did this come from*. None answers *who checked it and what did they conclude*. `derived_model` is the near-miss and is still upstream of verification — it says an LLM wrote this solution, not that anyone confirmed it. That is exactly why 1,093 rows carry it while ~9,546 questions are described as audited: two different facts, never stored in the same place.
  - **A REGISTRY WAS THE OBVIOUS ANSWER AND WAS REJECTED.** The `src/lib/relevance/config.ts` precedent (reviewed scope in TS, exceptions in the DB) works for the syllabus-fit screen because ~95% of questions pass, so only the failures carry information. Solution review is the inverse: **the per-question verdict is the expensive artifact and it already exists** — a blind re-derivation agent judges every question and today that judgement is flattened into a paragraph and discarded. Three concrete failures decided it: the scope unit is not stable (chapter for `/solution-cleanup`, `source_file` for JEE, bank-wide for a probe, single-question for report triage — one registry axis cannot hold all four); a chapter marked reviewed in May silently claims every question uploaded into it since (the [[default-becomes-assertion]] shape); and a registry cannot carry a verdict, so the moment you want one it has become a table with extra steps.
  - **`reviewed_content_hash` is the load-bearing column, and it came from re-reading 0040's own comment** ("so staleness is detectable"). A review describes the question AS IT WAS; without the fingerprint a confirmed row keeps asserting itself about text that has since been repaired. Two corollaries the implementation forced: a corrective script stamps the hash the row holds **after** its own edit (the edit is the review's own output — stamping the pre-fix value would make every fixed row born stale), and an unknown current hash counts as STALE, never fresh.
  - **Append-only is not ceremony — the NCERT Integrals cross-check already produced an overturned review.** An agent recommended flipping our (A) to match the printed key; source-verification reversed it (the agent had `options:[]` from a dump bug; the page shows our (A)=6 is right and the NCERT key is wrong). An UPDATE erases both the reversal and the reason — which is the single most valuable line in the record, and the thing [[audit-probe-symmetry]] keeps re-learning. Current belief = newest row per question, not the only row.
  - **TWO THINGS DELIBERATELY GET NO ROW, for the same reason.** A HELD key dispute in `grounding/commit.ts` is a flag, not a verdict — the adjudication that follows (`apply-key-fix.ts`) is what concludes one. And **a structural probe pass (`audit:keys`) records nothing**: it checks three defect shapes and is blind to a plain wrong key, so stamping every scanned row `confirmed` would turn ~40k unexamined questions green — precisely the failure that shipped 169 false coverage verdicts on the exam spines. Only an adjudicated flag earns a row.
  - **Report triage never infers a verdict from a status.** "wont-fix" on an answer complaint IS unambiguous (a reporter said the answer was wrong, we rejected the claim ⇒ `confirmed`) and is derived without asking; "resolved" is ambiguous — flip, repair, or rewrite — so it records only if the admin picks one, and the picker is offered only for answer-affecting categories. The server re-derives this from the **stored** category and never trusts the client, or a caller could stamp `confirmed` on a question nobody adjudicated. Fixed a pre-existing no-op on the way: the reports "Save note" button called `apply(currentStatus)`, which the same-status guard swallowed — so notes had never saved.
  - **Pilot scope (user's call): `scripts/grounding/*` only** — the run-label convention is easier to change before five callers depend on it. Read surface `npm run reviews:report` (coverage by exam/subject · per-run correction rate · stale reviews), counts from the `get_chapter_facets` aggregate on the **anon** client so RLS scopes them to PUBLIC, never a row-derived `.select()`. First run against prod: **40,720 PUBLIC questions, 0 recorded**, and the report says in those words that a 0 means *not recorded*, not *unexamined*. **Backfill deliberately NOT done** — and the good news found while scoping it is that **53 machine-readable review artifacts are already committed** (37 `*.mcq-verify.json` carrying real question ids, 16 `*.crosscheck.json` carrying `ref` + verdict + derivation). Those are parseable evidence; Decisions-log prose is not, and reconstructing from it would put a plausible guess where the table promises a record. Hence `source: 'live' | 'backfilled'`. Verified: 42 unit + 6 RLS integration tests, full gate PASS, 625 prerendered `.html` (no de-caching), advisors show only the expected `rls_enabled_no_policy` INFO. **Owed: the signed-in browser click-through of the triage verdict picker** — `/dashboard/reports` is auth-gated `ƒ`, so the gate proves it compiles, not that it lays out.

- **2026-08-11 — mh-sb-11 chapters 2 + 3: Angle and its Measurement (99 q) + Sets and Relations (165 q) → Class 11 at 387 q PUBLIC. The Part-2 path is proven, and band planning was rebuilt after it stranded questions twice.**
  - **Both chapters: 0 OUR-ANSWER-WRONG.** MCQ keys TRIPLE-confirmed on Angle (my blind derivation = agent's = the book's printed `BBADDCBBAC`) and double-confirmed on Sets (`CDDCADCDDD`; that chapter prints no MCQ key). `board:lint` · `audit:text` · `audit:keys` clean on both. Errata now **31 items across 3 chapters** (24 misprints + 7 answer-key errors).
  - **THE PART-2 PATH IS RETIRED AS A RISK.** Sets and Relations was deliberately taken third — ahead of higher-weightage chapters — precisely because nothing had exercised it: `answersPdf` resolving into volume 2, per-chapter `answerPages` inside it, and the book's numbering RESTARTING at Ch.1 so refs read `Ex 5.1` (colliding by name with Part-1's Straight Line, safe only because each chapter is its own `source_file`). All three held; the answer block sat exactly where `config.ts` predicted (`ans-214`, printed p.205). **Choosing a chapter to retire a structural unknown beat choosing it by weightage.**
  - **THE EXPENSIVE LESSON — plan transcription bands from a `Solution :` SCAN, never from section banners.** A banner scan (`SOLVED EXAMPLES` / `EXERCISE N.M`) sees only the BOXED blocks, but this book embeds worked examples with printed solutions directly in the theory, and those are `solved` rows too. Ch.5 carries **29** `Solution :` markers of which only ~18 are in banners. Consequence: **four separate "belongs to nobody" gaps** across Ch.1 and Ch.5 — `1.1 SolvedEx.8`'s solution tail, the whole octagon example `1.2 SolvedEx.6`, ~11 theory-embedded §5.1/§5.2 examples, and `Ex 5.2 Q9`. Every one was recovered ONLY because agents were told to REPORT on territory they did not own; that instruction turned out to be doing more work than the boundaries themselves. Now codified in `scripts/mh-sb-11/README.md`: scan for solution markers → cut bands at BLOCK boundaries (never page breaks) → name the split point in both prompts → theory-embedded examples get SUB-SECTION-scoped refs (`5.1.6 SolvedEx.1`) so they cannot collide with the boxed block's bare `<N.M> SolvedEx.<n>`, which the book reuses.
  - **`Ex 5.2 Q9` is the judgement call worth keeping:** skipped by the transcriber under the brief's "skip Activity boxes" rule, but it is a NUMBERED exercise question merely tagged `[Activity]`, with 8 determinate sub-answers. The rule targets open-ended classroom tasks with no determinate answer. Ingested as 8 set-siblings. **And the book's own pre-filled row in it is WRONG** — it marks `a+b even` on ℕ as NOT transitive, when that relation is congruence mod 2, i.e. an equivalence relation. The single cell the book fills in as its worked example teaches the wrong answer.
  - **Two more substantive book errors, both verified by the maintainer rather than taken on an agent's word:** (a) Angle answers "ratio of radii" with the ratio of the ANGLES, **twice** (60:75→4:5 and 65:110→13:22, where equal arcs make radius inversely proportional to angle, so 5:4 and 22:13) — two independent agents each found one, and only seeing them together identified it as a systematic slip rather than a typo; (b) `5.1.6 SolvedEx.1(c)` derives `(x−4)/(x−3) ≤ 0` correctly then prints the `≥ 0` region, so x=3.5 satisfies the inequality yet is excluded and x=5 fails yet is included.
  - **`apply-errata.ts` MCQ mirror hole CLOSED — the same defect this session found sitting live in `stateboard`.** The mirror tested `typeof hit.solution === "string"`, which is false for an MCQ fragment row (an MCQ carries a key, not a solution), so the bracket landed in the DB only and the next re-commit would revert it. It now CREATES the field and warns loudly when a bracket fails to mirror anywhere. Sets' `Misc I Q9` bracket would otherwise have been lost. The identical hole is confirmed live in `scripts/stateboard` (3 of its 76 brackets, all MCQ rows) — logged in the SUGGESTIONS backfill ledger with a 360, awaiting permission since that is shipped work.
  - **Also banked:** an authoring agent caught its own **id permutation** (rows shifted onto neighbours' ids) via a `ref`→`id` pairing diff — an id-SET check passes such a shift perfectly, since it is a permutation, not a loss. `SOLUTION_BRIEF.md` now requires diffing the PAIRING, not the set.

- **2026-08-10 — NEW exam `mh-sb-11` (Maharashtra State Board Class 11) + `scripts/mh-sb-11/` pipeline; pilot chapter Trigonometry - I → 123 q PUBLIC (merge `0b1843b`).** Class 11 had ZERO presence before this: no exam row, no chapter in any config, no data files — the header phrase "Class 11/12 textbooks" described the source shelf, not shipped work. Sources were on disk all along (`C:\tmp\PYQPs\MHT-CET\State_Board\11th\Maths` — 18 pre-split chapter PDFs, ~410pp, both volumes carrying a full ANSWERS section).
  - **Own exam, NOT a fold into `mh-hsc-12`** (which is literally named "…Class 12"). Class 11 is not a board year ⇒ no PYQs ever ⇒ the `mh-sb-9` shape: `practiceOnly` + `boardExam`, its OWN `Mathematics` subject (`subjects.exam_id` is NOT NULL). `lib.ts` re-exports the stateboard pure core VERBATIM; `/board` + the exam picker are registry-driven and picked it up with no extra wiring.
  - **THE LOAD-BEARING FINDING — transcription is VISION-ONLY, and it is MEASURED, not assumed.** The text layer looks clean (~2k chars/page of readable prose) and is unusable for math: in Ch.2, **`√` occurs ONCE in 21 pages and `/` ONCE**, while the pages carry dozens of surds and stacked fractions — so the extractor returns `3 2` for √3⁄2 and `= - 3` for tan 120° = −√3. Greek letters, degree signs and relational operators DO survive, **which is exactly what makes the text layer look trustworthy**. Same failure as Class-9 Real Numbers; recorded in `config.ts`, the README and the transcription brief so a later chapter cannot quietly reach for `dump-text.ts`. Generalises: *a text layer that renders prose perfectly can still be arithmetically lossy — measure a glyph you KNOW is on the page before trusting it.*
  - **Subtopics are the BOOK's own section headings**, pulled from the `syllabus_concepts` MH-State-Board XI spine (227 sections, extracted from these very books) rather than invented — the shipped `mh-ssc-10-text` decision. That spine also supplied canonical chapter names, several already matching the MHT-CET bank's (`Circle`, `Conic Sections`, `Straight Line`, `Measures of Dispersion`).
  - **Answer pages live in the WHOLE-BOOK PDFs** (Part 1 idx 225-241, Part 2 205-221) while transcription runs off the pre-split chapter files, so `answersPdf`/`answerPages` are per-chapter ranges into the book. Render them to **`out/_answers/<id>/`, a SIBLING of `out/<id>/`** — `render.ts` rmSync's the latter (the `mh-sb-9` `dump-text` trap). Also: **Part 2 restarts at Ch.1**, so refs follow the BOOK, never the spine's continuous numbering.
  - **Pilot verification — 0 errors on our side.** 123 q (28 solved · 85 exercise · 10 MCQ) across 6 subtopics and 6 `/board` blocks (123 routed, 0 orphans). **MCQ keys TRIPLE-confirmed** — my blind re-derivation = the transcription agent's = the book's printed key `BAABABDCBB`. Cross-check gate: **0 OUR-ANSWER-WRONG across all 123** vs **12 book defects** → errata brackets. Both answer-key errors are *self-refuting on the page* (Ex 2.1 Q3 prints `cos 4° > 0` AND `cos 4° < 0` in one line; the Misc II table prints `tan 315° = −1` beside `cot 315° = 1`) — I re-read both pages myself and checked all 90 cells of the two answer tables, finding exactly the one flagged error. `board:lint` · `audit:text` · `audit:keys` clean; full gate PASS.
  - **THREE pipeline defects found by RUNNING the pilot, two of them silent-data-loss class.** (1) **`apply-errata.ts` mirrored brackets to the DB but not to the source** — its inherited filter matched only `.solutions.json` / `.s<digit>` / `.misc.json`, so 7 brackets on solved examples lived ONLY in the database and the next re-commit would have reverted them; drift closed and *proven* by re-merging. (2) **`merge.ts` globbed every `<id>.*.json`** and ingested `blind.mcq-verify.json` as "10 questions" — only a coincidental duplicate ref in `errata.json` stopped 10 garbage rows entering the source of truth. Both now exclude scratch artifacts BY NAME. **Shared lesson: an allow-by-exclusion glob over a directory that also holds generated dumps is a silent-corruption hazard, and it fails OPEN.** (3) A Class-9 theory erratum rode along in the copied `errata.ts` and was appearing in the Class-11 publisher report.
  - **My own error, worth recording because every gate passed it:** the errata brackets were authored **double-escaped** (`\\(\\pi` where KaTeX needs `\(\pi`), which satisfies `findLatexImbalance` and every probe but renders as literal text. Caught only by reading the stored rows back. *Verify LaTeX by inspecting what landed in the DB, not by whether the writer reported success.*
  - **`tests/exam-context.test.ts` pinned "Std 11 resolves to null — no Class 11 corpus" as an HONEST GAP**, which this change intentionally falsified. Before editing I confirmed `stdsForBoard`/`getExamForBoardStd` have **no consumers** in `src/` or `scripts/` (a forward-looking written-paper-picker API), so nothing suddenly offers Class-11 papers. MH Std 11 → `mh-sb-11`; CBSE Std 11 stays null.
  - **Ops:** a 9-page vision band hit the stall watchdog (its file was already written, so nothing was lost); the 3-page band was comfortable — **use ~5-page bands for the remaining 17 chapters**. The gate's first run reported **exit 0 while actually FAILING**; only the `GATE_RESULT` sentinel revealed it. **Owed:** `/board` reveals answers on click, so stem rendering is proven (271 KaTeX spans, 0 raw LaTeX) but **answer rendering — including the one pipe-table answer — needs a browser click**. Remaining scope: 17 chapters ≈ 1,500-2,300 q, best ordered by measured MHT-CET weightage (Trig-I 99 · Limits 93 · Trig-II 90 · Determinants & Matrices 50 …). See [[mh-sb-11-ingestion]].

- **2026-08-09 — Sitemap `<lastmod>` made TRUE per URL (migrations 0072 + 0073), and the three canonical-less pages closed — prompted by a Search Console "Duplicate without user-selected canonical" email whose real content was somewhere else entirely.**
  - **THE EMAIL WAS ABOUT 1 PAGE. The coverage export said the thing worth reading: 20 indexed, 942 "Discovered – currently not indexed", 13 crawled-not-indexed, 3 blocked, 3 redirects, 1 duplicate.** Most of the investigation went into modelling a *mass*-duplication mechanism (`/login?next=` variants, the 72 chapter-slugs repeated across exams) before the export arrived and showed the count was one. **The generalisable error: an alert's severity is DATA, and it was one click away in the tool that sent the alert.** Get the magnitude before modelling the mechanism — see [[diagnose-from-data-not-assumptions]].
  - **The 1 duplicate page, identified by exhaustive audit of the live site:** `/login`, `/signup`, `/pricing` were the ONLY public 200-routes with no canonical, and `/login` + `/signup` are `"use client"` pages whose server HTML is an empty shell — **278 visible characters, 0 `<h1>`, 0 `<form>`, 0 `<input>`, byte-identical to each other** (`BAILOUT_TO_CLIENT_SIDE_RENDERING`). Identical content + nothing declaring a canonical is exactly this GSC reason. **The cause of the missing canonical is structural: a `"use client"` module cannot export `metadata`** — hence new `login/layout.tsx` + `signup/layout.tsx` carrying `robots: { index: false, follow: true }` (nobody searches for a sign-in form), and a canonical on `/pricing`, which stays indexable. `/login` also left the robots.txt Allow list — **deliberately still crawlable, because a Disallow would PREVENT Google from seeing the noindex.**
  - **THE REAL FIX — `<lastmod>` was a single build clock stamped on 984 of 988 URLs.** `sitemap.ts` read `const now = new Date()` once and reused it at 33 of its 34 sites, so every deploy announced that the entire site had changed — including the ~54% of pushes that touch only docs or ingestion scripts. Google discounts a lastmod it learns not to trust, and with 942 URLs queued that date is the ONLY lever for saying "this chapter is the one that changed". Now: **397 landing pages carry `max(created_at)` of their own chapter's PUBLIC questions; 93 notes/guide chapters carry their `_data` directory's git commit date; index/hub pages carry the newest of their children; only genuinely-aggregate pages (home, `/browse`, `/questions`) keep the build date.** Live result: **40 distinct lastmod days, up from 4 — with 0 of 397 landing pages falling back.** Pure core `src/lib/seo/lastmod.ts` (12 TDD cases; the load-bearing one is that ancestor matching is SEGMENT-WISE — a naive `startsWith` would hand `/guide/nda-maths-advanced` the date of `/guide/nda-maths`). Also removed from the sitemap: `/login` (now noindexed) and `/guide` (a 307 to `/guide/nda` — listing a redirect asks Google to index a page that does not exist; it was one of the 3 "Page with redirect"). **`/guide` was deliberately LEFT a 307 rather than promoted to a permanent 308** — a 308 is aggressively cached and hard to reverse, and the file's own comment says `/guide` becomes a real exam picker once a second exam ships.
  - **MIGRATION 0072 WAS WRONG AND 0073 REPLACES IT — the failure mode is the lesson.** 0072's aggregate was bank-wide: `max(created_at) … group by chapter_id` over every PUBLIC pyq row. Measured on prod it walked **25,026 rows for 6,144 buffers in 5,430 ms**, because `questions_question_kind_idx` is `(org_id, question_kind)` and carries neither `chapter_id` nor `created_at`, making every row a heap fetch. At 5.4s it sat right at the anon statement timeout and **failed INTERMITTENTLY** — a standalone probe returned all 421 rows while the same call from the running app was cancelled. **The bug was invisible because the call site destructured `{ data }` and dropped `error`**: JEE Mains, MHT-CET and MH-SSC-10 silently fell back to the build date, i.e. the exact defect being fixed, reintroduced through a swallowed error. Caught only by noticing that the exams which resolved were precisely the practice-only ones. **An index was the obvious fix and was rejected**: `questions` is the most heavily written table here and this project has already spent a session tracing disk-IO exhaustion to write amplification — paying a permanent write cost on every ingest to speed a read that happens once a day behind an `unstable_cache` is the wrong trade. Instead the aggregate is **scoped to one subject** (0073), riding the existing `questions_filter_idx` and joining the per-subject `Promise.all` that was already there: **88.7 ms for the largest subject, ~61× faster, no new index, no extra round-trip**, and a slow subject can now only cost its own dates. The error is logged rather than swallowed. **Two rules banked: measure before indexing (the 2026-08-01 lesson, reconfirmed — but note it cut the OTHER way this time, and only the measurement distinguishes the two cases); and a best-effort lookup must still SURFACE its error, or its failure mode is indistinguishable from the bug it fixes.**
  - **En route, the JEE subject-name trap fired again**: a first EXPLAIN filtered `subjects.name='Mathematics'` and returned 0 rows — JEE's subject is **`Maths`** — producing a meaninglessly fast plan that would have "proved" the query was fine. A measurement over an empty set is not a measurement.
  - **Not the problem, and worth recording so it is not re-investigated:** the 398 `/questions` landing pages are correctly built. Uppercase paths, trailing slashes and arbitrary query strings all resolve to a self-canonical clean URL; 72 chapter-slugs repeat across exams but on distinct URLs each carrying its own canonical. **And the 942 "Discovered – currently not indexed" is not a defect at all** — it is crawl rationing for an 11-day-old set of pages on a low-authority domain (~20 impressions/day). No configuration change manufactures crawl demand; this work makes the crawl budget land on the right pages once it arrives.

- **2026-08-09 — The unattributed disk spill traced to SUPABASE'S OWN METRICS EXPORTER, not to any app query; clearing `pg_stat_statements` (4,868 entries → 7) STOPPED IT COMPLETELY — ~13 GB/day to zero; migration 0071 turns the store's size into a watched gauge.** The 2026-08-07 tracker had been warning `spill-unattributed` — 8.5 GB written in a 16-hour window with only 8 MB attributable — and this session chased it down.
  - **THE FINDING: the thing doing the spilling is the thing that measures spilling, and it excludes itself from measurement.** The culprit is `postgres_exporter` (Supabase's Prometheus collector, running as `supabase_admin` on a connection open since 2026-07-28). It reads the ENTIRE `pg_stat_statements` store once a minute to build its metrics; the store had grown to **4,868 entries carrying 3.8 MB of query text against `work_mem` of 2,184 kB**, so every scrape sorted more than fitted in memory and spilled **5,802,298 bytes — byte-identical every time — twice a minute, around the clock**. That is ~13 GB/day on a **164 MB** database. Its own queries are absent from `pg_stat_statements`, which is exactly why three separate query-level investigations found nothing.
  - **Ruled out by measurement before accusing anything** (each cheap, each decisive): every tracked query moved <1 MB and none went backwards between snapshots; `dealloc` was only 12; `pg_cron` is not installed; `track = 'top'` does not hide nested statements (their usage rolls up). What finally named it was `log_temp_files`.
  - **The diagnostic, and the permission wall in front of it.** `log_temp_files` cannot be set from SQL here — the `postgres` role is **not** superuser on Supabase (`ALTER DATABASE … SET` → *permission denied*), a limit I asserted a plan around before checking. The working route is the **CLI**: `npx supabase --experimental postgres-config update --project-ref <ref> --config log_temp_files=4MB --no-restart` (flags go AFTER the subcommand; the API rejects a bare number — `4MB` is accepted because it is a string). Applied by config **reload**, no restart, no dropped connections; reverted the same way after ten minutes. Read-only alternatives failed and are worth recording: `pg_ls_tmpdir()` polling caught **nothing** in 25 s because each file lives well under a second, though the filename carries the owning PID and would have identified it instantly on a hit.
  - **The fix is a one-liner, and it was total.** `pg_stat_statements_reset()` took the store **4,868 entries / 3,800 kB → 7 / 5,611 bytes** and evictions 12 → 0. **Spill went to ZERO**: `temp_files` and `temp_bytes` are cumulative, and 56 minutes after the reset both stood exactly where they had 5 minutes after it (5 files / 26 MB) — across a window that included a full `prepush` (690-page build + ~2,800 tests). At the old rate that window should have produced ~80 files. ~13 GB/day → 0.
  - **AN INTERIM CONCLUSION OF "IT HALVED" WAS WRONG, AND THE WAY IT WAS WRONG IS THE LESSON.** It was measured over **5.4 minutes** — below the tracker's own `MIN_RATE_WINDOW_HOURS` floor, i.e. a window this very codebase already refuses to extrapolate from; the caveat was even stated and then the conclusion drawn anyway. The 5 files it saw were almost certainly **self-inflicted**: `npm run db:health` plus a verification call to `collect_db_health()` plus several `pg_stat_statements` scans, each of which spills ~5 MB by design (documented in [[db-health-tracker]]). **When measuring whether an intervention worked, the diagnostic tooling is part of the system under test** — either idle out the window or use probes that cannot spill (`pg_stat_database` alone is safe; anything touching `pg_stat_statements` is not). The "files arrive in PAIRS" observation was real; the inference built on it — that only one of each pair was the store read — did not survive a longer window.
  - **A number this file has been quoting is wrong by an order of magnitude, and the shape of the error is the lesson.** The 2026-08-05 entry called the `/browse` wide-sort "99.4% of the entire database's temp writes". It was 99.4% **of the `pg_stat_statements`-tracked subset** — and that subset is only ~10% of reality: **114 GB tracked vs 1,145 GB in `pg_stat_database`**. So ~90% of this database's spill has always been invisible to statement-level attribution. The `/browse` fix was still real and is confirmed dead (those ten retired query shapes read 0 calls / 0 bytes across a full window), but it was the largest thing we could SEE, not the largest thing there was. **Corrected in the Recurring-pitfalls bullet.**
  - **Migration 0071 folds the lever into the tracker** (`statements_tracked` / `statements_max` / `statements_evictions` + `statement-store-full` at 80%/95% and `statement-store-evicting` at any eviction). Rationale: the cost scales with the store's SIZE, the store refills in ~3 months (over half its entries were one-off ingestion/audit queries run exactly once — 2,615 of 4,865), and without a gauge the next session would simply repeat this whole investigation. Eviction earns its own rule because what a full store discards is **this tracker's own input**, so the reports silently under-count. Columns are **nullable on purpose** — the 20 pre-0071 snapshots have no reading, and a `DEFAULT 0` would render as a healthy 0% measurement that was never taken. Gauges deliberately survive a stats reset (they are the reading that tells you whether to reset again), which is asserted by test.
  - **Verification:** 52 dbhealth tests green (9 new flags + 4 new delta), full `prepush` PASS, **624 prerendered `.html`** on disk (the [[shell-component-decaches-site]] check). The reset also exercised the tracker's own refusal path end-to-end — the next report declined to measure the window, naming the reset, instead of printing a negative delta.
  - **CLOSED — no residual, no support ticket.** The 56-minute zero-delta window settles it; nothing further to diagnose. What remains is maintenance, now automated: the store refills in ~3 months (2,615 of 4,865 entries were one-off scripts run exactly once), and 0071's `statement-store-full` warning is what will say so. Should the spill ever return, the recipe that named it in one pass is in [[db-perf-diagnosis]].

- **2026-08-07 — Database health tracking shipped (migrations 0069 + 0070, `npm run db:health`, daily GitHub Action) — and the investigation that motivated it CLOSED with the opposite answer to the one assumed** (merge `da90f24`, full gate green + a second independent pass by the pre-push hook).
  - **THE PROBLEM, stated once so it stays stated: every Postgres load counter is CUMULATIVE with no time dimension.** `pg_stat_statements` will say a query has spilled 1,129 GB since the stats were reset (2026-05-08); it cannot say whether any of that was today. That is not academic — after the `/browse` wide-sort was fixed on 2026-08-05 there was **no way to confirm from the database that the spill had stopped**, because the lifetime total keeps the old number forever. Two snapshots subtract to a real rate; one snapshot cannot. Everything else here follows from that.
  - **The `/notes` question, answered by measurement, and the answer is "do nothing".** `loadWorkedExamples` is the highest-volume query left (1,041,940 calls). Two complete builds, both exit 0: **cold (`.next` deleted) = 437 calls ≈ 3.6 MB; warm (cache intact, 690/690 pages, 624 `.html`) = ZERO.** Next's fetch cache spans builds, so `unstable_cache` would buy nothing locally or on Vercel. **The user's proposal to inline the examples into the page was declined on correctness, not effort**: the bank changes constantly (~235 wrong keys flipped, solution-cleanup sweeps, stem repairs), so a frozen copy would keep teaching a superseded answer with no lint catch, and RLS withdrawal (a flawed question set PRIVATE) would stop working. If ever wanted anyway, the right shape is a generated snapshot + a drift probe — never hand-inlined content. Only cold CI builds still pay (~437 calls), judged not worth a `.next/cache` CI step: ~100–250 MB/month against an 11.48 GB read, and caching build inputs slightly weakens the gate.
  - **The "unexplained 8,600 calls/day gap" was an ARTIFACT of the measurement window, not a mystery.** The 91-day window starts 2026-05-08 — the project's beginning — and for most of it EVERY page was dynamic, because the `AppHeader` de-caching bug was not fixed until 2026-07-29. The 11,479/day average therefore describes a period dominated by an already-fixed defect. **Generalisable: a lifetime average is not a current rate, and the older the window the more it describes history you have already repaired.** En route I proposed a ×3-builds-per-push explanation and had to withdraw it — arm 2 showed local and Vercel builds are warm, so only CI is cold.
  - **Attribution was VERIFIED, not assumed** — I suspected `/browse`'s two-phase fetch shared the query shape and was wrong: the 1M-call statement selects exactly `loadWorkedExamples`' column list (name-only joins, no `image_url`/`set_id`/`question_format`), while `queryQuestionsByIds` selects wider and is a different queryid. Checking cost one query and would have invalidated the whole analysis if skipped.
  - **The design centre of the tracker is REFUSAL: it never reports a number it cannot stand behind.** Three ways the subtraction lies, each yielding `null` or an explicit flag rather than something plausible — (1) `pg_stat_statements` reset (cumulative deltas null, point-in-time gauges still valid); (2) a single query entry evicted and re-created, counters restarting while the global reset timestamp does not move (`suspectedReset`, under-count disclosed); (3) **the window too short to extrapolate — measured, not hypothetical: two snapshots two seconds apart reported "268 GB/day" from 7 MB of real activity**, most of it the collector's own query.
  - **TWO RULES CAME FROM FIRST CONTACT WITH REAL DATA, and both refuted the plan.** (a) **Collecting queries by LIFETIME rank misses exactly what the tool is for** — the first run showed 29 MB of database spill with nothing in the list to explain it, because a query that starts misbehaving today has a small lifetime total by definition. 0070 records every query that has *ever* spilled (rare: 100 of 4,477), plus a `spill-unattributed` rule, since silence when the database spills and no tracked query accounts for it reads as "all clear". (b) **Per-call spill alone is unusable** — the widened collector produced 20+ warnings, every one a hand-run analysis query that executed ONCE and spilled ~5 MB. Volume is what made `/browse` an incident, so a query must also clear a total-for-the-window floor. **The general lesson: a monitoring threshold cannot be calibrated from first principles — it has to meet real data before it is trustworthy, and the first contact usually shows it is too noisy, not too quiet.**
  - **Also corrected a number this file has been quoting:** the largest chapter is **531 PUBLIC questions**, not 346, against the PostgREST 1000-row cap. The 346 counted PYQs only; the cap applies to whatever a `.select()` returns, so 531 is the honest distance — ~2× away, not ~3×.
  - Scope discipline: **report-only, no alerting** (thresholds nobody has calibrated against a week of real data should not page anyone), and the collector's own cost (~2.7 s, ~8 MB spill per run) is documented rather than optimised. 33 TDD cases; the gate earned its keep by catching a fixture field that vitest alone passed over. Runbook `scripts/dbhealth/README.md`. **First genuine 24-hour window lands the morning after ship — that is when "did the /browse spill actually stop?" finally becomes answerable.**

- **2026-08-07 — Three database performance fixes (migrations 0067 + 0068 + the `/notes` chapter-landing count), merged separately.**
  - **0068 — the optional-parameter anti-pattern, and it is worth recognising by sight.** A `language sql` body with `where (p_x is null or col = p_x)` is planned ONCE, generically: the planner cannot prove the parameter non-null, so every predicate is demoted to a filter and the index is unusable. Proven with `EXPLAIN (GENERIC_PLAN)` — **Seq Scan cost 7,941 vs 541** for the same query with literals. Fix is `plpgsql` + `RETURN QUERY EXECUTE … USING` (a one-shot plan). **`plan_cache_mode` does NOT fix it** (tested, no effect). `public.match_chunks` (0040) is the only other function in the schema carrying the pattern; left alone deliberately — 2 calls ever, ~1 ms, the tutor that would consume it was never built.
  - **0067** indexes the `/notes` subtopic counts and rewrites `get_pyq_years` as a loose index scan.
  - **The `/notes` chapter landing fetched ONE ROW PER QUESTION** (`.select("subtopic_id").in(...)`) and tallied in JS to render about five integers — 9,858 bytes for 170 rows on NDA Maths Matrices, on all 83 chapter landings, every build. Now aggregates through the existing `get_subtopic_facets` RPC: **9,858 → 423 bytes, same numbers**, no new migration. **The correctness fix is worth more than the bytes**: the old query inherited the PostgREST 1000-row cap, so once a chapter crossed 1000 PYQs the weightage counts would have silently UNDER-reported. **The trap avoided**: `get_subtopic_facets` is CHAPTER-scoped and returns every subtopic, where the old query asked only for notes-REGISTERED ones — passing it straight through would have inflated a number the page renders, so `subtopicCountsFromFacets` narrows it back and that is the first thing the tests assert. Two siblings deliberately NOT changed: the concept-tags tally (a fifth the payload, nowhere near the cap) and the `head: true` drill count (0-byte body, 0.181 ms after 0067 — caching it would buy a staleness contract for no gain).

- **2026-08-07 — The MATHEMATICS syllabus map shipped END-TO-END in one session: registry → both book spines → three exam spines → all 308 rulings → both derived/authored edges. `/dashboard/syllabus/maths` is live and complete** (pushed `b035c45..134f4ca`, full gate green). Third subject after Chemistry/Physics, and the first to go from nothing to fully-adjudicated in one pass.
  - **Phase 0 — the first subject where the three banks disagree on the subject literal.** JEE's subject row is `"Maths"`; NDA/MHT-CET say `"Mathematics"`. Exactly two seams join the bank by subject name (`ingest-bank-spine`, `loadOldSyllabusByExam`) and both would have SILENTLY dropped JEE — a 196-of-308 spine and an empty dead-chapter scan, no error. Fix: `bankSubjectNames` on the registry entry, resolved INSIDE the loaders via `bankSubjectNames()` so no caller can forget it. `liveFromYear: 2024` measured off the bank (H&D + Mathematical Reasoning stop at 2023, the 2025 JEE rationalisation; Properties of Triangle last fired 2024 on 11 q — reads live, correctly undecided).
  - **Phase 1 — SB spine (408/33, `dump_sb_maths_sections.py`): the Balbharati Maths years are TWO PARTS that both restart at Ch.1 §1.1**, colliding on the spine's unique key. Decision: renumber Part 2 continuously (XI +9, XII +7) — NCERT's own Part-2 convention. Print defects recorded, never smoothed, each verified on the rendered page: TWO duplicate section numbers in print (Trig-I 2.1.3 twice; XII Trig-Fns 3.3.5 twice → `b`-aliases), the 2.2.x→2.9 numbering jump, a whole misnumbered run (3.3.5-3.3.7 solution-of-triangle inside the inverse-trig sequence, then 3.4.6-3.4.8 with no 3.4 ever printed), "4. 1" with a space INSIDE the number, 10 heading-only typos ("allied angels", "funcation", "Carstesian", "Elementry", "trignometric"…) via stale-guarded TITLE_FIXES, and 13 titleless/math-set headings transcribed from the page. 24 "missing" parents are the book's own style (skips the parent, opens at x.y.1 — corpus-scanned + page-verified).
  - **Phase 2 — NCERT spine (244/27, `dump_ncert_maths_sections.py`): every extraction miss was SILENT.** The killer: NCERT sets the number/title separator space in its OWN span — a whitespace-span filter joined "7.1Introduction" and **Binomial Theorem extracted 0 sections with no error** (a 0-section chapter is now an always-blocking problem). Also: title = the LEADING BOLD RUN (sub-headings run into body text with no separator), bare numbers, trailing dots ("5.5."), an internal space ("10. 5" hid Ellipse entirely), 3 colored-ITALIC titles the bold rule correctly rejects, and chapter titles verified against each chapter's own opening pages at run time (all 27 passed; "Reprint 2025-26" = rationalised).
  - **Phase 3b — all 308 subtopics ruled (JEE 112 · CET 85 · NDA 111) by 10 agent slices + the NCERT→SB edge (128) by 3 more, against RULING_BRIEF + a maths addendum.** Integrity: **5 withheld blind controls, 5/5 matched** (Bayes full/full with the exact refs; Cayley-Hamilton partial/partial; H&D not/not; Mean Deviation not; Shortest-Distance full via the predicted XII:6.3.x); **~25 falsifiable claims independently re-run, all reproduced**; four commit guards green on every batch. Session-limit kills mid-wave lost NOTHING — agents resume from transcripts via a follow-up message, and later waves were told to write their output file EARLY.
  - **THE HEADLINE, the Physics conclusion amplified: the State Board out-teaches rationalised NCERT across Maths.** NCERT no longer teaches **the PLANE** (117 JEE PYQ with no NCERT home — ch.11 ends at shortest-distance), trigonometric equations, EITHER triple product (a 71-PYQ CET subtopic), Mathematical Reasoning, Bernoulli/binomial distribution + the random-variable pmf/mean/variance sections, quadratic-equation theory (the chapter is still TITLED "…and Quadratic Equations"), A.P./H.P./special-sums/infinite-G.P., determinant manipulation properties, the inverse-trig identity toolkit, conic tangents/asymptotes, sine/cosine/projection rules, Rolle/MVT, n(A∪B), the power set. **NCERT wins in exactly four places** (mean deviation, image-of-a-point, the FTC derivative form, remainder-by-expansion) **plus one-variable linear inequalities — which the SB teaches NOWHERE** (its only inequality work is the 2-variable LP chapter). **Neither book teaches**: Cayley-Hamilton (NAMED by a 27-PYQ CET subtopic; "Cayley" appears once in 2.37M chars, as a history note), Vieta's/nature-of-roots (Class 10 in both boards), telescoping, L'Hôpital, idempotent/orthogonal/rotation matrices, H&D, regression/correlation.
  - **Evidence traps that fired and are now in the brief-lore:** stale NCERT chapter INTROS still promise deleted content (four chapters); "Rolle" prefix-matches "rolled" dice; "telescop" hits are the telescope instrument in conics; SB prints "orthocenter" (-er); an appendix pointer ("Refer A.1.3") is not a taught section; a term appearing only in optimisation EXAMPLES (sphere) is not coverage; 'bijective' is ZERO in SB (it says "one-one and onto").
  - **`audit-directions` was subject-blind and MANUFACTURED 129 contradictions the moment the Maths pointers landed** — a Maths "XI:4.2" colliding with a Chemistry verdict row, plus a Chemistry-only retired-chapter list. Same bug class Phase 0 fixed in the loaders, now in a PROBE: fixed (subject-scoped keys + per-subject retired set) → 0 real contradictions. **Rule: when a second subject's data lands in a shared-key table, re-run every standing probe — the subject-blind ones misfire loudly and the count looks like YOUR regression.**
  - **Derived edges same-day:** `derive-board-status --subject=maths` → 903 matrix rows (CBSE 226 · JEE 224+9 · CET 223 · NDA 221 cited SB sections; uncited stays unassessed); the authored NCERT→SB edge lifted the alignment table 0 → **153 NCERT cells / 89 backed pairings**. Cross-year skew is systematic (NCERT XII relations/matrices/determinants/probability = SB Std XI; NCERT XI 3-D intro = SB Std XII). See [[syllabus-map]] + [[corpus-search-for-coverage-rulings]]. **Owed: the signed-in browser render** (`ƒ` page — loaders proven via smoke, render not).

- **2026-08-06 — Test isolation: `npm test` moved OFF production onto a dedicated test Supabase project, after leaked test fixtures were found live on `/mock`, `/browse` and `/quiz` — two real students had sat a 1-question fake mock.** The user spotted a stray "Mock RLS published 418149d9" mock under MHT-CET/2099; analysis traced it to interrupted test runs (Aug 1, 03:12–08:07 UTC — killed gates/session limits strand `afterAll`) and found THREE structural blind spots in the org-keyed teardown sweep: **org-less platform tables** (`mock_tests`, `quizzes` — no org to cascade from), **fixtures written INTO the real org** (`/api/sync/mock`'s whole job is writing to LWS Pune), and **`@test.invalid` emails** vs the `@test.local`-only sweep. The "one universal signal is `org_id`" rule from 2026-06-11 was thereby disproven. Cleaned all residue (2 mocks + 3 attempts, 7 sync questions + 4 taxonomy nodes, 2 users, 5 quizzes incl. one PUBLICLY ROUTABLE at `/quiz/pubtest-…`, 12 atoms, 2 smoke-test rows, **9,305 test `rate_limits` rows accumulated since May** — `export-route-kind.test.ts` has no afterAll at all).
  - **The structural fix (Layer 1) instead of better sweeping (Layer 2):** a signature-registry/startup-sweep design was analysed in full and then deliberately DROPPED — with a dedicated test DB, leaks land somewhere harmless and a blunt `testdb:reset` beats any signature matching. Test project `rjwuwmrzkyergflmmfxq` in a **separate free Supabase account** (org-level quotas: test traffic must not share prod's already-strained egress/disk-IO budgets). `tests/setup.ts` + `tests/helpers/testdb.ts` resolve `.env.test.local`/`TEST_SUPABASE_*` and **hard-refuse any project ref not on an ALLOW-list** (fails closed on unknown refs — the ~10 lines that permanently replace the sweep apparatus). `tests/global-teardown.ts` shares the resolution (it read `.env.local` directly and would have swept PROD while workers wrote to the test project).
  - **The migration replay was itself a probe: it caught migration 0021 (question_concept_tags) applied to prod via MCP in May but never committed** — the same defect as 0066; prod was not rebuildable from the repo. Recreated from the live schema (0056 pins the policy name it must create). `testdb:migrate` is now the standing check for this class.
  - **Reader/writer split:** fixture-writers (~200 files' worth of suites) run on the test DB; **prod-contract readers** (guide taxonomy-resolution, cross-link-integrity, live-count shapes, `/go` name-mode — `tests/prodContractFiles.ts`) still run read-only against prod via `npm run test:prod-contract` (own vitest config, NO globalSetup so the sweep never runs off a read-only pass; in `prepush:quick` + a CI step). The full-suite run against the test DB empirically confirmed the split — every "Exam not found: NDA" failure was a prod-contract file.
  - **Seed = what tests BORROW, discovered empirically:** taxonomy.json (MHT-CET only — NDA etc. were auto-created by uploads in prod, never seeded), a hand-seeded NDA branch (Statistics → the Central-Tendency subtopic + "Matrices & Determinants" → "Determinant Properties, Operations, and Sums" — filter-facets + edit-apply-tags + notes-reports resolve these BY NAME), an "LWS Pune" org **with an ADMIN member** (`applyMockSync` 400s without one), ≥150 PUBLIC questions (browse-query asserts the historic seed floor; Physics stems carry the word "lens" for its full-text test), 3 COMPLETED upload_jobs. Also stabilized the four "borrow any PUBLIC question" tests with `order created_at asc` — on a small DB an unordered `limit(1)` picks a parallel run's transient fixture that its `afterAll` deletes mid-test (FK 23503); and pinned mocks-rls's exam borrow (the unordered pick is why the leaked fixture had landed under MHT-CET).
  - Verified: 66/66 migrations, table lists byte-identical to prod, main suite **2,715 tests green on the test project**, prod-contract **183 green on prod**, typecheck clean. CI now needs the `TEST_SUPABASE_*` secret trio (documented in ci.yml) — **`npm test` in CI fails until they're added.** Runbook: `scripts/testdb/README.md`.

- **2026-08-06 — JEE Mains CHEMISTRY COMPLETE: 259 → 3,455 q PUBLIC across all 88 papers on disk. The exam is now Maths + Physics + Chemistry, every sitting 2021-2026.** Chemistry was the last subject; it had sat at legacy-2021-only since the exam was added. 45 papers ingested this run, the majority through the **BLIND lane** — no trustworthy key, so every answer is independently derived and the source key is never shown to the solver.
  - **The SAFE lane earned its keep on a paper my own triage called SAFE.** 2023-apr11's ENTIRE Q141-Q150 NAT key block is displaced by +1 — each key is the NEXT question's answer. Proven on three rows before touching anything: Q148 was keyed **224** for "maximum lone pairs on a central atom", which is impossible (the true max is 3) and is in fact Q149's 2.24 L written as 224 x 10^-2; Q149 was keyed 4, which is Q150's answer; Q141 was keyed 1, which is Q142's Kc. **Q143 is the trap worth remembering — its displaced value happens to EQUAL its correct value, so it agrees, and that agreement is pure coincidence.** Nothing in the numbering can see a shift INSIDE a complete block; only re-derivation can, which is why `triage.ts` documents itself as a report and not an oracle.
  - **Thirty source answer-key errors** caught and corrected across the run, each re-derived against the printed options rather than taken on an agent's word. Two recurring shapes: the printed solution contradicting its own printed letter (micellisation, where the body reads dS>0 and dH>0 while the heading prints the exactly-backwards option), and a dropped digit in the key table (dT = 0.82 K keyed as 2 instead of 82). Papers with ZERO errors are common and were reported as such — the 1-2 per paper rate is a prior, not a quota.
  - **~60 questions skipped rather than guessed**, and the reasons are specific, not "hard": two options printed byte-identical (chemistry unambiguous, LETTER not); a stem whose own numbers are self-contradictory; 63 g of a polymer whose repeat unit weighs 106; a Kc question where the paper's own solution derives 0.02 while its key says 50 (the reciprocal) AND the stem demands a "nearest integer", so neither value is defensible.
  - **The 2021 compilations are NOT 30/30/30 and the extractor assumed they were.** p16 prints 17 Physics / 24 Chemistry / 20 Maths, so its "Chemistry" dump was full of integrals, determinants and 3-D planes. Caught by scanning the dumps for cross-subject terms BEFORE dispatching agents at them. `retag-compilation.ts` now reads the truth the source states outright (`**PART-II CHEMISTRY**`) and refuses unless banner totals reconcile exactly with the record count. Checked, and did NOT touch, the already-shipped p11/p12 and the shipped Physics of p13-p16 — all were classified BY CONTENT in an earlier pass, so no backfill was owed. **Verifying that before raising an alarm mattered; the inference that shipped data must be contaminated was wrong.**
  - **Four latent pipeline bugs, every one a SILENT failure.** (1) `skip[]` is paper-global but keyed on question number, so an earlier subject's pass suppressed this subject's questions after the re-tag. (2) Both assemblers globbed `<id>_sol_*.json` and merged them — readdir is alphabetical, so `_sol_physics.json` **overwrote** `_sol_chem.json` for shared numbers, clobbering real answers with that pass's `skip:true`. This was the actual cause; fixing (1) alone changed nothing. (3) `assemble-blind`'s can-synthesize gate read options from the agent JSON only, so an option block restored by hand was invisible to the check it existed to satisfy. (4) Content-free math zones — `\(\ \)`, a trailing `\ `, and `\(\\)` (which parses as a lone backslash, since the closing `\)` claims the second one) — each reduce to a bare backslash that KaTeX rejects, taking the whole stem down, while `scan-flip` passes them because the delimiters balance. Together 1-3 were dropping **46 real questions** behind a bare "(skip)" that read like a deliberate call.
  - **New standing tools:** `coverage.ts` (reconciles a subject BOTH ways — it was validated against Physics, which correctly reported 0 not-ingested), `triage.ts` (SAFE vs BLIND per paper), `retag-compilation.ts`, `unskip-retagged.ts`, `apply-stem-fixes.ts`, `apply-option-fixes.ts`, `repair-broken.ts`, and `AGENT_BRIEF.md` (the two prompts, pulled out of the dispatch messages where they had been retyped for ten waves). `dump-subject.ts --blind` withholds the key, because an agent that can SEE a shifted key rationalises toward it rather than solving past it.
  - **Completion is verified, not asserted:** 88 papers on disk, 88 ingested, 0 not-ingested, 0 in-DB-but-not-on-disk; every short paper's gap accounted for by its own `skip[]` except 5 already-logged stragglers; 3 rows withheld PRIVATE, all deliberate; 1 KaTeX-broken row across 10,634 JEE questions, and it is the pre-existing PRIVATE one. **Caveat worth stating plainly: BLIND-lane answers have no external check** — they are derived, not verified against a key, because no usable key exists. That is weaker evidence than the SAFE papers carry, and it covers most of this run. `audit:keys` flags 381 of 2,539 MCQs, but 325 are `IMAGE_OPTIONS` (what a drawn option looks like, not a defect); of the 50 `SOLN≠KEY` I sampled ~26 and every one was a probe false positive, in three families the probe cannot distinguish — distractor-dismissal tails, match-list mappings, and statement-set answers where the trailing letters are STATEMENT labels rather than option letters. **Not all 50 were checked individually.** See [[jee-mains-ingestion]].

- **2026-08-05 — Egress measured and traced to BUILDS, not traffic: the gate's `next build` is now conditional on the changeset (`918f0b5`), removing ~3.9 GB/month with no loss of coverage.** Follows the query fix below; the Supabase free tier reads 11.48 GB against a 5 GB allowance, 96.4% of it PostgREST. **⚠️ SUPERSEDED 2026-08-13 — DO NOT PLAN AGAINST THESE FIGURES. The project is on SUPABASE PRO: 250 GB included, 1.73 GB used, 0 GB overage (~0.7%, 100–390 MB/day).** Egress is a non-issue with ~145× headroom, and it stopped being one at the plan change, not at this fix. The "11.48 GB / 5 GB" pair above is a free-tier reading preserved for the narrative only; it was quoted as current fact in a later session and sent an hour of work at a problem that no longer existed. The conditional build remains worth having (it is also build minutes and CI time) — just not as an egress remedy. **The only meter still worth watching is Vercel Hobby Active CPU.**
  - **THE ARITHMETIC, and it closes a loop left open on 2026-07-29.** A `/questions` landing-page response is **37 kB** (25 rows, 1,506 bytes/question, measured); a full build prerenders 689 pages of which ~317 query Postgres ≈ **25 MB**; git says **145 pushes to main in 30 days**, each triggering **THREE** builds — pre-push hook, CI, Vercel deploy. 145 × 3 × 25 MB ≈ **10.9 GB against 11.48 GB observed.** Builds essentially ARE the egress; scripts, audits, MCP and real users are the remainder. This also explains the flat ~200 MB daily plateau the first egress analysis flagged as unexplained: 200 ÷ 25 ≈ 8 builds ≈ a 3-push day. **It looked "quantised" because it literally is a count of builds** — a plateau in a usage chart is a hint to look for a fixed-size repeated unit, not a mystery baseline.
  - **THE FIX: 78 of those 145 pushes (54%) changed nothing the compiler reads** — pure ingestion data and docs — so their builds *could not have failed* and ran twice each anyway. Build is now conditional in both gates off ONE shared rule (`scripts/lib/needsBuild.ts`, spec `tests/needs-build.test.ts`, CLI `scripts/needs-build.ts`, consumed by `.githooks/pre-push` + a `if:` on CI's Build step) so the two cannot drift.
  - **The polarity is the whole design: an allowlist of SKIPS, never of builds.** Inert = `*.md` · `tests/` · `scripts/` · `supabase/` · `generated-papers/` (verified: nothing under `src/` imports any of them, and only a LEADING segment matches so `src/scripts/…` is still app code). Anything unrecognised — a new top-level dir, a new config file, a rename — **builds**. Inverted, this would be a silent hole in the gate that widens as the repo grows; as written it can only ever be too cautious. Fail-safe extends to the plumbing: an unresolvable base (new branch, all-zeros SHA, shallow clone, missing git) reports "build needed", and **CI needs `fetch-depth: 0`** or every run fails safe and silently undoes the saving.
  - **This beat the option I had recommended.** I first proposed dropping the local pre-push build outright (~3.6 GB) — but that trades away real protection, and measuring the src-vs-data split showed the conditional version saves **more** (~3.9 GB, because it covers CI too) at **zero** coverage cost, since it only skips builds incapable of failing. **Measuring the changeset split before choosing changed which option was correct.**
  - **Supabase Pro was ruled out by the user** (right call for now — it buys ceiling, not cause), and the reasoning inverted vs the entry below: capacity is the wrong answer for a defect that grows with the bank, but a reasonable one for a fixed quota. **Also on the table and cheaper than anything built: push less often** — 430 commits across 145 pushes is ~5/day; batching to 2/day would alone take builds to ~4.5 GB/month.

- **2026-08-05 — `/browse` was writing ~14 MB to disk on EVERY page load: `queryQuestions` sorted the full row shape to keep 25 rows. Split into two round trips; temp writes for that path go to ZERO (`fa9b627`).** Opened by a Supabase "running out of Disk IO Budget" email, and it turned out to be the same defect behind the ECONNRESET gate failures and part of the egress overage.
  - **THE DEFECT: a sort's payload is every SELECTED column, and this query selected everything.** `queryQuestions` fetched `text`, `context`, `solution`, four lateral joins and the options aggregate in the *same* query that carries the `ORDER BY` — so Postgres sorted ~22,000 rows at ~922 bytes each in order to keep 25, an `external merge` spilling **13.9 MB per call**. `pg_stat_statements` attributes **99.4% of the whole database's temp writes to that one query shape** (112 GB / 30k calls in the tracked window; **1,097 GB lifetime**, against a 144 MB database). Fix: an id-only query carries the filters + ORDER BY + range, then `queryQuestionsByIds` fetches the wide shape for the 25 surviving ids. Sort payload ~922 → ~30 bytes.
  - **Measured, `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, TIMING OFF)` as `anon`: `external merge Disk: 13.9 MB` / 178.8 ms → `top-N heapsort Memory: 27 kB` / 33–48 ms, 0 temp.** The full gate then passed with **zero** ECONNRESET / statement-timeout / SIGTERM signals, 234 test files green, 623 prerendered `.html` (nothing de-cached).
  - **TWO of my own prior diagnoses were WRONG, and the wrong one was written into the code.** (1) The 2026-08-03 entry and a comment in `query.ts` both asserted the query "runs in ~13 ms" and blamed pure contention — that measured an index lookup, not the statement the app issues, and it sent the fix to `experimental.cpus: 4` + `staticPageGenerationTimeout: 180`, throttling the symptom. (2) The egress analysis concluded "no single runaway query is responsible" — true of bytes returned, false about the machine's health. **A plan-level measurement (`pg_stat_statements` / `EXPLAIN`) would have caught both; an EXPLAIN of a hand-simplified query caught neither.**
  - **`count: "exact"` was the obvious suspect and was NOT the problem** — PostgREST already compiles it to its own narrow `pgrst_source_count` CTE, measured at 10 ms as an index-only scan. Hand-splitting it would have been a no-op. Checking before building saved the wrong fix.
  - **This host's clock makes `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` lie by ~50×** (3,219 ms with per-row timing vs 178.8 ms with `TIMING OFF` for the identical statement). Any latency number from this database needs `TIMING OFF`; `Sort Method` and `temp written` are plan properties and stay trustworthy under load, which is why they are the acceptance criteria here rather than wall time.
  - **Deliberately NOT adding a covering index** for the sort: it would help only some filter combinations, and every index adds write amplification on ingestion — more of the same Disk IO the fix exists to protect.
  - **Incidentally fixed a live bug:** `queryQuestions` selected `numeric_answer` but never mapped it, so JEE Section-B NAT questions rendered a **blank answer on `/browse`** while `/saved` (which uses `queryQuestionsByIds`) showed it correctly. Sharing the mapper fixes it and deletes ~60 duplicated lines. **The general shape: two functions returning the same view-model with two hand-written mappers will drift, and the drift is invisible on the surface nobody re-checks.**
  - **Ordering was entirely unasserted** across 8 existing browse-query tests — precisely the invariant a two-phase fetch breaks. Three tests added (base contract, across a page boundary, subtopic teaching order) and **proven to go red** when the order restoration is removed. Egress is untouched by this fix (both phases still return 25 rows) — see the ROADMAP entry for what remains.

- **2026-08-05 — The Physics syllabus page was rendering an unchecked blank AS A CHECKED CLAIM, and its chapter matrix was entirely empty. Both fixed, plus the 238-section NCERT→State Board edge authored.** Triggered by the user asking "are these tables not updated?" — they were not stale; two of the three edges the page reads had never been authored for Physics.
  - **THE DEFECT: the alignment table printed "not in NCERT" on all 318 rows, and the page's own caption calls that "a checked claim".** Nothing had been checked — the NCERT→State Board edge had ZERO rows for Physics (Chemistry has 416). It was demonstrably false and contradicted by data already in the same database: the page said *Dimensional Analysis → not in NCERT* while NCERT XI carries 1.4/1.5/1.6 on exactly that, and my own JEE ruling cites them as `full`. **`AlignmentTable` now takes `ncertMapped`**, derived from the payload already in hand, and renders **"not mapped yet"** when a subject has no such edge — with the caption switching to say a blank is *an absence of review, not a finding*. Chemistry is untouched.
  - **THREE EDGES, and only one had been authored.** (A) exam subtopic → both books — 502 rows, done 2026-08-04. (B) NCERT section → State Board — 0. (C) State Board section → per-exam status — 0, which is why every cell of the chapter matrix showed "?". Naming them separately is what made the fix tractable; "the tables are stale" would have sent me looking in the wrong place.
  - **(C) was DERIVED, not authored — 1,346 rows, no new judgement.** `derive-board-status.ts`: a State Board section cited by a live exam subtopic is required by that exam. Two deliberate conservatisms, both learned the hard way this week: the verdict is **`partial`, never `full`** (a pointer proves the exam asks *something* in that section, not that all of it is needed), and **an uncited section gets NO ROW** rather than `not`, because "no exam cites it" conflates *not required* with *not sampled by the bank* — writing `not` there would be exactly the confident unreviewed verdict of [[default-becomes-assertion]]. The script **refuses to overwrite an existing column** without `--force`; verified it fires on Chemistry, whose 4,315 rows were hand-authored years earlier and would have been destroyed.
  - **(B) was AUTHORED — 238 top-level NCERT sections, five agents, every verdict from book text.** 232 full · 5 partial · 1 not. The alignment table went from **0 NCERT cells to 340** (of 414 rows), and the matrix's CBSE column filled by inverting the same edge. Every falsifiable claim was re-run by the reviewer and matched exactly (`banked` 15, `Aristotle` 5, `heat engine` 75 vs 13, `blackbody` 56 vs 6, `stationary wave` 46); one agent overstated a single count (`magnetisation` "zero" in the State Board chapter — actually 6 against 14 for the -z- form) and the note was corrected before commit.
  - **A silent-misapplication bug caught in `commit-bank-rulings.ts` BEFORE it ran.** `bySection` was keyed on `section_no` alone — safe for an exam spine, where `JEE-001` is globally unique, but **NCERT has a section "1.1" in Std XI *and* Std XII, and both are titled "Introduction"**. Half the rulings would have landed on the wrong year's row, and the subtopic-name guard could not have seen it because the names match. Now keyed by `class|section_no`, with a ruling able to carry `class`; where it does not, an ambiguous `section_no` is a **loud refusal** rather than a silent pick. All three Chemistry files re-validate byte-identical (298/244/94).
  - **What the mapping shows.** The books are organised very differently and a chapter-title comparison would miss nearly all of it: **Gauss's law is taught a year earlier** in the State Board (XI:10.8); electrostatics, current electricity, magnetism and semiconductors each **split across both years**; vectors live in a **Mathematical Methods** chapter NCERT has no counterpart for; friction is in **Mechanical Properties of Solids**; Work/Energy/Power has **no chapter at all**, folded into Laws of Motion; and **transformers are taught in Electromagnetic Induction, not the AC chapter** — independently reconfirming the 2026-08-04 finding from the JEE side. Genuine State Board gaps: **Power** (no section defines it; `kilowatt` and `horsepower` are ZERO), **rotational equilibrium / principle of moments / mechanical advantage** (all ZERO — corroborating yesterday's NDA "Simple Machines" partial), **applications of elastic behaviour** (`girder`, `buckling` ZERO), the **reflecting telescope** (`Cassegrain` ZERO) and **C = 3R for solids** (`Dulong` ZERO in both). Against those the State Board is the fuller book on Reynolds number, the hydrostatic paradox, blackbody radiation, heat engines, the cyclotron, the toroid, metre bridge and potentiometer, hysteresis, and the coefficient of restitution.
  - **The -s-/-z- spelling split was confirmed three more times** and is now unambiguous: `quantisation`/`quantization`, `magnetisation`/`magnetization`, `polarisation`/`polarization` — each NCERT-only in one form and State-Board-only in the other. Plus naming traps that would each have produced a false `not`: `law of inertia`, `relaxation time`, `mirror formula`, `power of a lens`, `forbidden` band and `equivalent solenoid` are all ZERO in the State Board while the content is taught under other words. See [[corpus-search-for-coverage-rulings]] + [[syllabus-map]].

- **2026-08-04 — Chemistry's MHT-CET and NDA spines ADJUDICATED for the first time: 169 subtopics / 2,427 PYQ, 338 cited link rows. 14 of NDA's 47 State Board verdicts (30%) were wrong, and every one of the 169 had been displayed as reviewed coverage having never been assessed.** Follows the table generalisation below, which exposed the problem: those two spines had a verdict for every subtopic and ZERO section citations.
  - **THE DEFECT: `ingest-exam-spine.ts` carries 226 authored rulings for JEE Mains and ZERO for MHT-CET and NDA, and defaults an unruled subtopic to `full`.** So all 169 read green. The Phase 3a entry below called that default "sound for Chemistry, whose author adjudicated the whole corpus" — true of **JEE only**, and now corrected in place. The generalisable lesson: **a default that is defensible for the case its author had in mind silently becomes an assertion for every case they did not**, and a green cell cannot tell you which it is. This is the same failure Phase 3a refused to ship for Physics, already shipped one subject over.
