  - **Result — NDA 33 full / 11 partial / 3 not (State Board), 21/16/10 (NCERT); MHT-CET 118/4/0 and 105/17/0.** MHT-CET's near-perfect State Board score is the expected result, not a finding — the exam is set on that syllabus — so its informative column is NCERT, where **17 subtopics have no home at all**.
  - **NCERT's rationalisation is now measured, not assumed.** `unit cell`, `edge length`, `packing efficiency`, `void`, `defect`, `vacancy`, `Schottky`, `Frenkel`, `doping`, `physisorption`, `chemisorption`, `Freundlich`, `colloid`, `Tyndall`, `micelle`, `thermosetting`, `elastomer`, `atom economy`, `nanomaterial`, `interhalogen`, `borax` and **`Boyle`** are ZERO across all 19 NCERT chapters while the State Board teaches each in a dedicated chapter. Solid State, Surface Chemistry, Polymers, Green Chemistry/Nanochemistry and both block chapters are gone; **NCERT Chemistry no longer teaches Boyle's law, Charles's law or kinetic theory anywhere.**
  - **But the reverse happens too, and it is the more surprising half.** NCERT is the fuller book on **Corrosion** (State Board `not` — 'corrosion' appears twice, both adjectival, while NCERT XII:2.8 teaches it with prevention), **Types of Reactions** ('combination reaction' is ZERO across the entire State Board corpus), **azeotropes** (10 NCERT hits, zero State Board), **solute association** (the i<1 benzoic-acid-dimer case), and **sp3d/sp3d2 hybridisation** (11 hits in NCERT's bonding chapter, zero in the State Board's, which defers d-hybridisation to Coordination Compounds a year later). Two teaching traps worth knowing: **NCERT uses molar volume 22.7 L and never says 22.4**, which the State Board uses throughout and NDA keys expect; and the State Board teaches Faraday's laws quantitatively but **never names them**, so a numerical is answerable and a "state Faraday's first law" recall item is not.
  - **METHOD — the tooling was the deliverable as much as the rulings.** `dump_chem_corpus.py` (51 chapters / 2.93M chars, per chapter because whole-corpus search once scored a real gap as covered) + **`search_corpus.py`** + a generated `chemistry-sections-handout.md` (1,288 citable sections, from the LIVE spine so it cannot drift) + **`RULING_BRIEF.md`**, the contract seven agents worked to. Three subtopics (Fertilizers, Cement, Paints) were adjudicated by the reviewer FIRST and withheld as blind controls; all three matched, and on Cement the agent was more careful than the reviewer, finding the glass-as-amorphous-solid thread and ruling `partial` where `not` was expected.
  - **The search tool's first version was wrong in a way that would have poisoned the whole batch.** Substring matching reported `cement` in 21 chapters — 17 hits in NCERT *Redox Reactions* — because it matched inside **displa-cement**. Whole-word matching then breaks `aldehyde`/`aldehydes`. The correct default anchors the START of the term only. **Five spelling traps were found by the agents and are now in the brief**: the two books split **-s- vs -z-** (`polymerisation` finds 9 NCERT hits and misses the State Board's 61); they use different reagent names (`bisulphite` vs `hydrogensulphite`, `bidentate` vs `didentate`); `Huckel` carries an umlaut in NCERT; and — a genuine **textbook error worth reporting to Balbharati** — the State Board spells **`Plank`** for Planck throughout its Std XI Structure of Atom chapter, 5 occurrences, the correct spelling appearing zero times.
  - **`commit-bank-rulings.ts` gained a fourth guard.** The DB has a 500-char CHECK on `note` and the write failed AFTER the dry run reported "all refs resolve" — false confidence, whole batch rolled back, nothing saying which note. The guard now mirrors the constraint and names the offender and its length during the dry run. See [[syllabus-map]].
  - **FOLLOW-UP, same day — `ingest-exam-spine.ts` RETIRED (1,186 lines deleted), so the defect cannot recur.** Fixing the data left the script that caused it untouched, and it was the only remaining `?? "full"` path. Three things had to be proven first, and one of them corrected my own account of the defect. (1) **The replacement reproduces the spine exactly** — a diff of what `ingest-bank-spine.ts` would compute against the live Chemistry spine came back **318/318 identical, 0 drift, 0 orphans**; both scripts number continuously across exams (JEE-001…149, MHT-150…271, NDA-272…318), so the earlier worry that refs would shift and orphan the rulings was unfounded. (2) **JEE Chemistry has ZERO defaulted rulings** — I expected ~72 and it is none: all 298 link rows either cite a section or carry a deliberate note, including the only 4 uncited-`full` rows, which are the two `Organic Reaction Mechanisms` catch-all subtopics whose notes say outright that a catch-all maps to no single section. So the original entry's "the author adjudicated the corpus" **was true of JEE**; what was false was extending it to MHT-CET and NDA, which had none. (3) **The port round-trips** — the 226 inline TS rulings were exported from the live spine to `data/chem-jee-rulings.json` and re-applied through `commit-bank-rulings.ts`; the ruling checksum was **byte-identical before and after** (298 rows, `9d1fab3b…`). Net: one exam-spine path, one ruling format, and all three Chemistry exams now sit under the same four guards as Physics.

- **2026-08-04 — The per-subtopic "where is this taught" table generalised from JEE-only to every exam: NDA (52) + MHT-CET (93) now render alongside JEE (106) on the Physics map — and a live cross-exam defect on the SHIPPED Chemistry page was fixed on the way.** No migration, no new rulings: Phase 3b had already authored every NDA and MHT-CET citation, so this was rendering, not authoring. `loadMappingRows` was already spine-parameterised and `MappingTable` already generic; the only blocker was `SPINE` naming JEE alone.
  - **THE DEFECT, and it was shipped: "old syllabus" was computed for JEE and then applied to EVERY exam's rows, matched by chapter NAME.** Exams share chapter names freely (27 collide in this data), so JEE dropping a chapter silently marked another exam's namesake dead. On the live Chemistry page that buried **MHT-CET Solid State (133 PYQ, examined through 2025), MHT-CET Surface Chemistry (40 PYQ) and NDA Chemistry in Everyday Life (10 PYQ)** — 183 PYQ of live content flagged as history. Worse than cosmetic: `loadExamSpineSummaries` drops old rows from `live` entirely, so each exam's coverage was computed over a SUBSET of its own subtopics (MHT-CET read 114 live of 122, NDA 45 of 47). All 10 rows are status `full`, so the gap LISTS were unaffected — the corruption was in the denominators. Fixed by keying the dead set BY EXAM (`loadOldSyllabusByExam`, one scan for all exams rather than one each); the single-exam function survives as a thin wrapper so the scripts are untouched.
  - **A shared key is not a shared fact.** The general shape worth keeping: "dead" is a property of an (exam, chapter) PAIR, and any set keyed on only half of a compound fact will silently over-apply. Physics happened to be clean — its one dead JEE chapter, Communication Systems, has no namesake elsewhere — so testing on Physics alone would have shown nothing.
  - **The render gate is CITATIONS, not row count — and that call was forced by the data.** Chemistry's MHT-CET and NDA spines carry a verdict for every subtopic but **zero section pointers** (122 and 47 rows), so a row-count gate would have rendered 169 rows of blank cells reading as "the State Board teaches none of MHT-CET Chemistry" — the exact opposite of true for an exam set on that syllabus. Unmapped exams are NAMED in a one-line note instead of silently omitted, preserving the map's core distinction between *reviewed and absent* and *never looked at*. The table appears by itself once someone authors the pointers.
  - **Copy is per-exam because a blank cell MEANS something different per exam.** MHT-CET gets "full State Board coverage is what should happen, not a finding — the informative column is NCERT"; NDA gets "a blank in BOTH columns means neither book has it, not that the row is unfinished — several are Class 9/10 general science". The old-syllabus sentence is **derived from the rows** (`rows.some(r => r.oldSyllabus)`) rather than asserted per exam, so it cannot go stale in either direction — it renders for JEE and correctly vanishes for NDA and MHT-CET, neither of which has a dead chapter.
  - **Two of my own bugs, both caught before they shipped.** A joined map key `` `${exam} ${chapter}` `` cannot be split back apart when BOTH halves contain spaces ("JEE Mains", "Solid State") → nested maps. And the verification script's clash detector counted "not in the other exam's dead set" as "live for that exam", so it reported Communication Systems as live for MHT-CET and NDA, **neither of which has that chapter at all** — absence from a dead set is not evidence of liveness. Also: a literal **NUL byte** reached the source through a shell heredoc (invisible, would have survived review), and a `\n` in another heredoc collapsed to a real newline breaking a string literal — reconfirming [[heredoc-backslash-eating]]: author code through the editor, never a heredoc.
  - Verified with `smoke-page-data.ts` (extended to all three exams + the live-gap summary + the render gate), which **proves rather than asserts** that the JEE dead set is byte-identical to what the pre-fix loader returned. Physics: JEE 106 · MHT-CET 93 · NDA 52, NDA's 4 blank-in-both rows exactly as ruled. Chemistry: JEE 149 only, the other two correctly suppressed. **Owed: the browser render** — the page is auth-gated and `force-dynamic`, so there is no headless proof; an anon curl is bounced by middleware before the route compiles ([[probe-must-reach-the-code]]).

- **2026-08-04 — Physics Phase 3b COMPLETE: NDA ruled, and with it ALL 251 bank subtopics across JEE + MHT-CET + NDA.** 502 cited link rows. **State Board covers 232 of 251 in full (92%), 12 partial, 7 not; NCERT fails 25.**
  - **NDA is the ONE exam where the State Board genuinely falls short, and the reason is structural, not a coverage failure.** It is a SERVICES exam whose General Ability paper draws on Class 9/10 general science and plain general awareness — Astronomy and Space, Energy Sources, Scientific Acronyms, Scientists and Discoveries are `not` in BOTH books because they are not Std XI/XII material at all. Six more are `partial` for the same reason: the **human eye** (4 passing mentions, no section — 'myopia'/'hypermetropia' ZERO in both), **simple machines** (levers and pulleys appear only as worked torque examples; no mechanical-advantage section), **Fleming's rules** ('Fleming' is ZERO in BOTH corpora — a Class 9/10 mnemonic), **SONAR**, the **human ear**, and domestic electrical devices. The ruling file's `basis` states this explicitly so a reader does not misread it: **`not` here means NOT IN THESE TWO BOOKS**, and several of these ARE in the Maharashtra Class 9/10 science books, for which this map carries no spine yet.
  - **Two more NCERT deletions the State Board still teaches**, both found by measurement: **Combination of Resistors** — 'resistors in series/parallel' is ZERO across all four NCERT volumes while the State Board keeps 11.8.2, and NDA asks 16 PYQ; and **scattering / rainbow / natural phenomena** — ZERO in NCERT against 42 mentions in State Board 9.9, feeding NDA's 29-PYQ Light Phenomena subtopic. Also **reverberation** (0 in NCERT vs its own State Board sub-sections 8.7.1-8.7.3).
  - **THE BANK-WIDE FINDING, now measured across all three exams: the State Board teaches materially MORE of what these exams ask than NCERT does.** 25 subtopics are absent from NCERT, and the State Board covers most of them in full — Logic Gates, Transistors, Special Diodes, Doppler, Potentiometer, LC Oscillations, Damped Oscillations, Earth's Magnetism, Pseudo Force, Relative Motion, Resistor Combinations, Communication Systems, scattering, reverberation, biprism, sonometer, end correction, Brewster, metre bridge, Reynolds. A CBSE-taught student attempting JEE or CET has real blind spots a State-Board-taught student does not.

- **2026-08-04 — Physics Phase 3b: MHT-CET COMPLETE — all 93 subtopics ruled, 186 cited link rows (163 full · 17 partial · 6 not).** Physics now has **199 of 251** bank subtopics adjudicated; only NDA (52) remains.
  - **ZERO State Board gaps, which is the correct answer and not a lazy one.** MHT-CET IS the State Board syllabus, so full coverage is the expected result; the single `partial` is the LR-circuit transient (growth/decay, time constant), which neither book teaches. The informative column here is NCERT.
  - **SIX subtopics NCERT does not teach, every one covered in full by the State Board — 127 PYQ**: Logic Gates and Boolean Algebra (39), Transistors (30), Doppler Effect (20), **Potentiometer (20)**, Special Diodes (11), Relative Motion (7). Potentiometer is the sharpest new one: **48 mentions across the State Board's ch.9 against ONE incidental mention in all of NCERT Physics.** Also newly measured as NCERT-absent or near-absent: **biprism** (0 in NCERT vs 13 in State Board ch.7 — and Young's/biprism/interference is CET's single largest subtopic at 73 PYQ), **sonometer** (0), **end correction** (0), **Brewster** (0), **metre bridge** (1 incidental), **resolving power** (2 mentions vs a State Board section plus three sub-sections).
  - **The `subtopic`-name guard fired on my own authoring error and was worth every line.** I copied the subtopic names out of SQL *with* their `(N PYQ)` suffix while the comparison strips it, so all 93 mismatched and the commit refused. Had the guard only checked `section_no`, all 93 rulings would have been written against positionally-correct but unverified rows.
  - **A THIRD variant of the negative-grep trap**, after `step-?up` (space) and `NOR` (English word): `newton'?s law of cooling` returned ZERO across both corpora because the books use a CURLY apostrophe. Both books have a section with exactly that title. The rule now has three independent confirmations: **vary spelling, spacing AND punctuation before calling a topic absent — and prefer a term that cannot occur as ordinary English.**

- **2026-08-04 — Physics Phase 3b COMPLETE for JEE: all 106 subtopics ruled, 212 cited link rows (186 full · 11 partial · 15 not).** Every ruling names the section that teaches it and was checked against the book TEXT via a new per-chapter corpus (`dump_physics_corpus.py`, 58 chapters / 3.3M chars), never against section titles.
  - **THE HEADLINE, and it is the opposite of what everyone assumes: the State Board covers JEE Physics BETTER than NCERT does.** JEE has **12** subtopics NCERT does not teach, and the State Board teaches **8 of them in full** — **Logic Gates (56 PYQ)**, Transistors (19), Doppler Effect (13), Pseudo Force (12), Earth's Magnetism (7), LC Oscillations (5), Damped Oscillations (2), plus Communication Systems (63 PYQ across two subtopics) where the State Board is `partial` and NCERT has nothing at all. That is **~114 PYQ of live JEE material a CBSE-taught student never sees and a State-Board-taught student does.** Against that, the State Board's own gaps are **three subtopics, 18 PYQ**: X-ray Spectra (3), Transient RC/LR (13), Defects of Vision (2) — and NCERT misses all three too. Every one of these was measured, not assumed: 'logic gate'/'truth table'/'NAND gate' are ZERO across all four NCERT volumes; 'Doppler' survives as ONE incidental mention; 'pseudo force' is ZERO.
  - **A title match is not evidence, and the corpus proved it repeatedly.** JEE Transformers has NO heading in the State Board AC chapter — a title pass calls it a gap — but XII:12.15 in ELECTROMAGNETIC INDUCTION teaches it in full (turn ratio eq 12.49, ideal-transformer power relation, step-up and step-down). Same shape for **Friction**, taught in Mechanical Properties of Solids rather than Laws of Motion; **Earth's Magnetism**, in Std XI ch.12 not the Std XII magnetism chapter; **Vector Addition**, in a MATHEMATICAL METHODS chapter NCERT has no counterpart for; and **Work/Energy/Power**, folded into Laws of Motion. Each is a place a teacher would look and not find.
  - **The spiral is real and pervasive.** Five subtopics cite sections from BOTH State Board years — Gauss's Law (XI states it, XII applies it), Semiconductor Diodes (XI p-n junction, XII rectifiers/Zener/LED), Bar Magnet, Collisions+Angular Momentum, Interference of Sound. Plus systematic cross-year offsets in the other direction: ray optics and EM waves are Std XI in the State Board but Std XII in NCERT; thermodynamics, oscillations, fluids and kinetic theory are Std XII in the State Board but Std XI in NCERT.
  - **`partial` is used on measured grounds, never as caution.** AM is taught but **modulation index** is absent; propagation is taught but the **antenna-range** relation is absent; Viscosity is full but **'Reynolds' is ZERO in NCERT**; Motion of Charge in a Field has both ingredients (F=qE, 2-D kinematics) taught separately and **never combined** — 'parabolic' and 'deflection' are zero in both State Board electrostatics chapters.
  - **Two of my own negative-grep claims were wrong before they were checked**, both the same trap: `step-?up` does not match "step up" *with a space* (the State Board carries MORE turns-ratio material than NCERT), and `NOR` matches the English conjunction "nor", which briefly made logic gates look present in six unrelated chapters. **A negative grep is the weakest evidence available — vary spelling and spacing, and prefer a term that cannot occur as ordinary English.**
  - Rulings are DATA (`data/phy-jee-rulings.json`), committed by `commit-bank-rulings.ts` under three guards each proven to fire against a corrupted fixture — the load-bearing one being that a recorded subtopic name must still match the spine, since `section_no` is POSITIONAL and a re-run of the spine ingest would otherwise apply a Transformers ruling to a different subtopic. **Next: MHT-CET (93) and NDA (52).**

- **2026-08-04 — Physics Phase 3a: the three exam-bank spines are LIVE — 251 subtopic rows (JEE 106 · MHT-CET 93 · NDA 52), via a new subject-parameterised `ingest-bank-spine.ts`.** Physics now carries all five spines (1,208 concepts). **ZERO rulings written — that is the point of the phase, not an omission.**
  - **THE DECISION: an unruled subtopic gets NO LINK ROW, diverging deliberately from the Chemistry script.** `ingest-exam-spine.ts` writes `status: cov?.status ?? (adj ? adj[0] : "full")` — an unruled subtopic becomes **`full`**. **CORRECTED 2026-08-04 — this entry originally said that was "sound for Chemistry, whose author adjudicated the whole corpus". That is true of JEE Chemistry ONLY.** The script carries **226 authored rulings for JEE Mains and ZERO for MHT-CET and NDA**, so all **169 MHT-CET + NDA Chemistry subtopics (2,427 PYQ) defaulted to `full` and were shown as reviewed coverage having never been assessed** — the very defect this decision was made to avoid, already shipped one subject over. Found while extending the per-subtopic table to those exams; being fixed by authoring them properly. The lesson generalises: **a default that is defensible for the case its author had in mind silently becomes an assertion for every case they did not** — and a green cell cannot tell you which it is. Reusing it here would have written **251 rows asserting the State Board fully covers Physics subtopics nobody has read**, contradicting 0065's core rule that an unassessed pair is the ABSENCE of a row. It is also the one error class no probe can catch: a wrong ruling is plausible, internally consistent and unfalsifiable by tooling — unlike Phases 1-2, where every defect was checkable against the printed page. So rulings are authored separately in reviewed batches and this script never invents one; the page renders unruled as blank/"?" rather than green.
  - **The prune is scoped to subject AND source.** `section_no` is POSITIONAL (`JEE-001`…), so a taxonomy change shortens the list and would strand the tail as ghosts. Scoping to subject matters equally: every subject's bank spine shares the same source names, so an unscoped prune would delete another subject's rows outright — the exact bug fixed in `ingest-ncert-spine` in Phase 0. `chapter_no` is also a true 1..N ordinal here, where the Chemistry script stores the index of a chapter's first ROW (1, 4, 9, …) — fine for grouping, meaningless as an ordinal.
  - Verified: counts reconcile exactly against the bank (JEE 28 ch/106 sub/3,482 PYQ · CET 24/93/2,221 · NDA 14/52/449); 0 CHECK violations; **0 link rows**; loaders read 106 JEE rows (104 live, 2 old-syllabus = Communication Systems) with 0 gaps, which is the correct reading of "nothing adjudicated". Chemistry byte-identical. **Next (3b): the rulings — per-subtopic, source-consulted, in batches.**

- **2026-08-03 — Physics Phase 2: the NCERT Physics spine is LIVE — 370 concepts across 28 chapters (Std XI 181 / 14 ch, Std XII 189 / 14 ch), via a new `dump_ncert_physics_sections.py`.** Physics now has both book spines (957 concepts total); `covered_by` pointers have targets to resolve against. Chemistry re-verified byte-identical. **No overlap between the two volumes of a year** (XI 1-7 / 8-14, XII 1-8 / 9-14) — the pre-start suspicion that Std XII Part_2 restated chapters 2/5/7/8 was figure references.
  - **SEVEN extraction bugs, all one root cause: the four PDFs are four typesetting generations that disagree on nearly every convention.** An absolute font-size floor returned **ZERO sections for all of Std XI** (it sets headings at *body size*, separated by weight alone, while Std XII separates by size); the fix is a floor measured against each volume's own body size. Then: Std XII's chapter-OPENER headings are plain `Bookman`, no demi/bold, so a weight-only test dropped **the first section of several chapters** (2.1, 3.11, 5.2, 12.2) while keeping the rest — so the rule needs EITHER signal (heavier weight at body size, or clearly larger at any weight). Also: `11.7THERMODYNAMIC STATE VARIABLES` has **no separator at all**, so `\s+` made the line fail to match entirely; Std XI Part_2 puts the number alone with the title on the NEXT line; `KEEP_UPPER` containing AND/OR/NOT (for logic gates) produced "Units AND Measurement"; and titles wrap across lines in both years.
  - **Span-level and plain-text reads fail on OPPOSITE things, so neither is "the right one".** Std XI sets chapter titles in small caps — one span per letter-run with no spaces — so the span join yields `COUNITSANDMEASUREMENT` with every word boundary lost, while plain text is perfect. Std XII paints each opener heading 4-5× for a drop shadow with the first letter painted apart, so plain text yields `2.1  I` ×4 and only the span read disambiguates. Each volume needs the read that survives ITS typesetting.
  - **The gap probe has a structural blind spot: it finds HOLES, never TRUNCATION.** Sections 1..7 are contiguous whether the chapter has 7 or 14, so every tail-loss bug was invisible to it. What exposed the rest was counts that failed to reconcile — and the reconciliation itself needed care: a crude "collect every bare N.M" probe reported massive undercounts that were **false**, because **NCERT numbers end-of-chapter EXERCISES `N.1, N.2…` identically to sections**. Checking the worst case directly (ch9: "31 listed" vs 7 extracted) showed the extras sit at 9.5pt — *below* body size — i.e. the exercise list, correctly excluded. The 7 was right.
  - **Two titles are genuinely unrecoverable from the text layer** (Std XII 5.2, 12.2 — small caps AND drop shadow together, painted as overlapping fragments at two sizes). Read off the rendered pages at 3× and transcribed from the image, following the Chemistry precedent, with a guard that a stale override **fails loudly** rather than overwriting a later-corrected title. Only 2 of 238 top-level titles needed this; the other 75 short ones ("Work", "Viscosity", "Radioactivity") are genuinely that short.
  - **One book defect preserved, not synthesised**: Std XI ch.2's opener box lists "2.5 Relative velocity", but the phrase appears NOWHERE else in the volume — the rationalised edition dropped the content and left the box stale. The spine records what the book teaches, so 2.5 is absent here exactly as it is there. **Casing is converted** (NCERT prints top-level headings in caps; the shipped Chemistry rows are Title Case, and the alignment table puts them side by side). `seed.ts` gained `--spine=ncert`; Chemistry's `ncertSeedFiles` is deliberately empty because `ingest-ncert-spine.ts` seeds it alongside its rulings. **Next: the exam spines (JEE 106 · CET 93 · NDA 52 subtopics).**

- **2026-08-03 — Physics Phase 1: the State Board Physics spine is LIVE — 587 concepts across 30 chapters (Std XI 255 / 14 ch, Std XII 332 / 16 ch), seeded from a new `dump_sb_physics_sections.py`.** `/dashboard/syllabus/physics` now renders a real chapter matrix; every exam column is correctly blank, since nothing is adjudicated yet. Chemistry re-verified byte-identical after the seed.
  - **Extract from the PER-CHAPTER PDFs, not the whole book — that one choice removes most of the noise.** Inside `05. Gravitation.pdf` the only valid section number is `5.x`, so every whole-book false positive (`8.31 J mol⁻¹K⁻¹`, `98.6`, `4.608 N`) is rejected structurally rather than by heuristic. Chapter number and title then come from the filename instead of a title page — the whole-book pass mis-read 6 of 16 Std XII chapter titles by picking up body text ("They have ability to take the shape of the").
  - **A heading needs TWO agreeing signals: the numeric shape AND the bold face.** Either alone fails — the body is full of figure refs in heading shape ("4.6(c) shows the view from the top"), and bold is also used for emphasis. Four extraction bugs were each found by reading the raw spans, never guessed: (1) numbering style is inconsistent *within one chapter* (`4.1. Introduction:` vs `4.5 Types of Forces:`), so the trailing dot is optional — missing this reported Laws of Motion as 4 sections instead of 35; (2) testing `spans[0]` for bold dropped real headings that open with a stray regular-font fragment (`6.6 Stress-Strain Curve:` is `[regular, BOLD]`) — those were exactly the missing 6.6 and 9.8; (3) a heading already ending in `:` is COMPLETE and must not absorb the next bold line, or "6.2 Elastic Behavior of Solids:" swallows its own body clause; (4) justified setting sometimes breaks a heading into **one word per line**, leaving the number alone (`3.10` then `Absorption,` `Reflection` `and`), so a bare number is a valid heading start.
  - **Loosening the pattern to fix (4) let three FIGURE CAPTIONS in** (8.22, 13.16, 16.26) whose numbers sit far past the real section count — and they were only visible because the gap probe flagged impossible runs like `ch16: missing 6..25`. **A "gap" was the symptom of a spurious row, not of a missing one.** Discriminator: no genuine section title in this book opens with a parenthesis. Also added a first-occurrence-wins guard, since Std XI ch.4 prints a bold `4.7:` figure ref that would otherwise overwrite the real §4.7.
  - **One printed-book typo, confirmed on the rendered page at 5× before touching it**: §3.3.3 prints "Equations of Motion for an Object **travellinging** a Plane". Corrected via an explicit `TITLE_FIXES` map keyed by (class, section) that **raises if the printed text ever stops matching**, so the override can't silently rot or misapply. Corrected rather than preserved because a spine entry is a NAVIGATION LABEL, not an answer key — nothing factual turns on it. A defect that changed a claim would be preserved and flagged instead.
  - **Verification before the write**: 0 numbering gaps, 0 duplicate refs, 0 titles violating the 0065 CHECKs, `seq` contiguous 1..N, and all 30 chapters opening at §N.1 Introduction. `seed.ts`'s new subject-agreement guard passed, the seed is idempotent, and the whole thing is reversible with one `delete where subject='Physics'`. **Next: NCERT Physics spine, then the exam spines.**

- **2026-08-03 — The syllabus map became MULTI-SUBJECT (Phase 0 of adding Physics): a subject registry + `/dashboard/syllabus/[subject]`, three real bugs fixed, Chemistry proven byte-identical.** Code only — no Physics spine is seeded yet, so `/dashboard/syllabus/physics` renders an empty-but-valid map. Deliberately shipped on its own, ahead of any authoring, because it also fixes a destructive bug that exists today.
  - **The finding that shrank the job: `SPINE`/`BOOK_OF_EXAM` name BOOKS, not subjects.** `syllabus_concepts` already carries `subject` in its unique key `(source, class, subject, section_no)`, so Physics reuses "MH State Board" / "NCERT" / "JEE Mains bank taxonomy" and **no migration is needed** — the plan's assumption that the spine constants had to become per-subject was wrong. Scoping a query needs BOTH columns: source alone merges two subjects' Ch.1, subject alone merges three books' Ch.1. The new `src/lib/syllabus/subjects.ts` therefore carries only what genuinely differs.
  - **`liveFromYear` had to be per-subject, and this is the load-bearing data finding.** The old `LIVE_FROM_YEAR = 2023` is Chemistry-calibrated: its seven rationalised chapters go silent after 2021. **Physics' rationalisation landed a cycle later — Communication Systems (63 PYQ) was still set in 2023 and has nothing since, while all 27 other JEE Physics chapters reach 2026.** Since the test is `lastYear < liveFromYear`, sharing 2023 reads a dead chapter as live; 2024 splits it 1/27 with a two-year margin. Verified live: the Physics loader returns exactly `Communication Systems`.
  - **THREE pre-existing bugs, one destructive.** `ingest-ncert-spine.ts` pruned stale rows on `source='NCERT'` with **no subject filter**, and `wanted` only ever holds the running subject's sections — so running the CHEMISTRY ingest would have deleted the whole Physics NCERT spine, rulings cascading with it. Plus `loadChapterConcepts` took no subject param (would merge two subjects' Ch.1 in the drill-down) and `gap.ts` read the table unscoped — its LINKS needed membership-filtering, not a column filter, because `syllabus_concept_exams` has no `subject` column at all.
  - **Two bugs introduced by the route change and caught in self-review**, both of which would have shipped a broken Physics page: chapter expand/collapse links were hardcoded to `/dashboard/syllabus`, which now redirects, so expanding a Physics chapter would silently bounce the reader to Chemistry (→ a subject-aware `basePath`); and the NCERT cross-year callout ("NCERT teaches Thermodynamics and Equilibrium in Class 11…") is a **counted Chemistry claim that is false for Physics** → moved to `crossYearNote` and **left unset for Physics, where absent means "not measured yet", never "the books agree"**. Writing a plausible note before counting is exactly the fabrication this map exists to avoid.
  - **`subject` is REQUIRED on all eight loaders, not defaulted** — that is what made the typechecker enumerate every caller instead of leaving silent `"Chemistry"` fallbacks. Scripts take `--subject=<key>` as a FLAG, never a positional, because several already read a positional meaning something else (`export-chapter-map-xlsx` reads argv[2] as "ncert"|"jee"|"all"); the positional readers now skip flags. An unknown subject **exits 1** rather than falling back, and the route **404s** rather than serving the Chemistry map under `/physics` — a fallback would read as "Physics has been mapped". `seed.ts` also now refuses to write when a seed file's own `subject` disagrees with the flag, since the upsert key includes `subject` and a mislabelled file would overwrite the wrong spine.
  - **Acceptance was "Chemistry must not move", proved from artifacts not reasoning:** the loaders' output diffed byte-identical against a stashed baseline (863 concepts / 32 chapters / 145 NCERT / 149 JEE / 7 old-syllabus / 5 live gaps). Build clean, 689 static pages, **623 prerendered `.html` on disk** (622 before, +1 for the new static redirect) — the [[shell-component-decaches-site]] check, confirming nothing de-cached. 82 syllabus unit tests. **Owed: the signed-in render** — `[subject]` is `ƒ` behind the middleware matcher, so an anon curl is bounced before the page compiles and proves nothing.
  - **Sources confirmed on disk before any of this** (`Physics/State_Board/Topics/Physics_11th.pdf` 223 sections/14 ch, `Physics_12th.pdf` 312/16, NCERT Physics XI+XII ~440), all with the same numbered-section structure the Chemistry extractor consumes. Physics' bank is far deeper than Chemistry's (**JEE Physics 3,482 PYQ vs Chemistry 259**). Two structural differences will shape Phase 1+: the State Board Physics book is a **two-pass spiral** (Std XI Ch.10 Electrostatics stops at Gauss; Std XII Ch.8 resumes at potential/capacitance — likewise current, magnetism, optics, semiconductors), so a chapter has two legitimate book homes and the one-dominant-chapter model hides one (`spiralChapters` flag is plumbed but UNUSED — the multi-home change is Phase 0.5, deferred until real rows exist to verify against); and the XI/XII divergence from NCERT is far larger than Chemistry's, making the `XI:`/`XII:` cross-year prefix roughly a third of rows rather than a dozen. See [[syllabus-map]].

- **2026-08-03 — The cross-book syllabus map became a product: a three-book alignment table, JEE chapters ordered along the State Board book, NCERT gaps, and every table folded behind native disclosure (merges `08c01cb` → `5a11ef4`).** The driving question is a teacher's: *"a CBSE student shows me an NCERT topic — where is it in my State Board book?"* Precision beats recall there, so most of the work was refusing to assert things the data does not support.
  - **THE LOAD-BEARING RULE: co-location is not equivalence.** NCERT and JEE both point AT the State Board, so it is tempting to join them through a shared section. Measured against the separately authored JEE→NCERT edge, that inference **agrees 129 times, INVENTS 39 and MISSES 25** — 77% correct, and a fabricated row looks exactly as authoritative as a real one. `buildAlignmentRows` therefore pairs ONLY on the authored edge and gives everything else its own row, repeating the anchor. That repetition is what buys the honesty: it removes any need to squeeze a many-to-many mapping into one cell. `scripts/syllabus/audit-alignment.ts` re-measures the split and **fails** if a paired row ever loses its backing.
  - **A verdict rolled up to a chapter is a lie waiting to happen.** The old "State Board content NOT required by exam X" view reported *Std XI Ch.8 not required by JEE* while JEE's LIVE s-Block chapter maps straight into it — the page contradicting its own JEE table one section below. Root cause was structural, not clerical: that view read the State Board spine's per-concept `status`, authored BEFORE the `covered_by` mappings, and nothing kept the two directions in agreement. A probe found **33 concepts marked "not required" that a JEE subtopic points at** (16 from live chapters). Rather than patch 16 rulings and leave two datasets free to drift, the rollup view was **deleted** and the surviving gap list reads from the mappings directly, so the contradiction cannot recur. **Those 16 stale `status` rows are still in the DB** — inert today, but any future view reading that column inherits the defect.
  - **Ordering: whole chapters along the book, never individual rows.** Grouping ROWS by State Board chapter reads down the book a teacher teaches but shatters "what does JEE ask in Amines" across the table; alphabetical keeps chapters whole but says nothing. Ordering whole CHAPTERS by their *dominant* State Board chapter (by PYQ weight, ties to the earlier chapter) keeps both. Dominance is deliberately not the earliest pointer: d-/f-Block touches Std XI Ch.2 with one subtopic but holds 39 questions in Std XII Ch.8.
  - **Two blanks that look identical mean different things.** "not in NCERT" is a checked claim (135 of 145 hand-verified); "not asked in the bank" only means no past question has been sampled. Worded differently on purpose — identical wording would quietly overstate the second.
  - **`/questions` prerender failures FIXED at the cause.** The query is ~13ms on an existing index; the failures were **contention** from 317 pages generating at once, and the 60s `staticPageGenerationTimeout` was SIGTERMing pages **mid-retry**, turning a recovering page into a hard build failure. `experimental.cpus: 4` + `staticPageGenerationTimeout: 180` in `next.config.mjs` took it from 4–6 statement timeouts + 2 SIGTERMs per build to **zero of both across two consecutive builds**. Closes the 2026-08-01 SUGGESTIONS item.
  - **37 of 47 `Organic Reaction Mechanisms` questions reclassified.** The catch-all was not laziness: those stems carry no topic signal (the chemistry sits in an image), so ingest agents bucketed rather than guessed. The **authored solution text** was the signal and was already present. 10 stay put — 4 Match-Lists, 2 genuinely three-chapter, 4 whose solutions are too thin. Paper JSONs written FIRST (they are the source of record; a DB-only fix is reverted by the next resync), then the DB **by primary key** — matching on `source_file` scanned 24k rows × 37 and timed out mid-run.
  - **Page size: 1,112 rendered rows → ~40.** All eight tables now sit behind native `<details>`, not a JS accordion — the page stays fully server-rendered, gets keyboard/screen-reader behaviour free, and browsers skip layout for the collapsed subtree. Each summary carries its row count. Known limit recorded in the component: Chrome/Edge auto-expand a closed `<details>` on Ctrl+F, **Firefox and Safari do not**.
  - **I reported a measurement as fact and it was wrong.** I sized the page at 855 rows having counted only chapter rows in the two matrices, missing that every top-level section renders under its chapter (`return [chapterRow, ...sectionRows]`). Real figures: 121 and 170, page total 1,112. The first collapse commit therefore left 323 rows visible, not the 66 I claimed. Corrected in the follow-up commit and in [[probe-must-reach-the-code]] — **count what the render emits, not what the data holds.**
  - **Migration 0066 was applied to production via MCP but its `.sql` file was never written** — found during this doc pass, recreated 2026-08-03 (idempotent). A fresh checkout would have lacked `covered_by`, which the entire feature depends on. The convention is append-only *files*; applying through the MCP does not satisfy it.
  - See [[syllabus-map]]. Also shipped: `loadNcertGaps` (21 NCERT sections the State Board misses, 4 tagged as a hole an exam already reports from its own side, leaving **6 gaps only this view surfaces**), the Std XII Chemistry syllabus tracker (16 chapters / 159 subtopics, State-Board-sourced with 6 NCERT plugs), and a Biomolecules data fix — `14.9 "shows Haworth formula of maltose"` was a figure caption parsed as a section; deleted after confirming zero pointers, and a probe found no others of that class.

- **2026-08-02 — Class-10 SSC gains a TEXTBOOK layer alongside its board PYQs: `scripts/mh-ssc-10-text/`, 5 History + Political Science chapters → 60 q PUBLIC, and a taxonomy realignment onto the book's own section headings.** The ask was "first 3 chapters of history and 2 chapters of polity", then *"You may want to align your taxonomy with that of textbook"*, then "pilot one", then "proceed". Analysing the source before writing anything changed the job three times.
  - **The load-bearing structural call: a second pipeline into the SAME exam and the SAME chapters, separated by `question_kind`.** Class 10 already had 1,519 board-PYQ rows across these very chapters. The textbook exercises are not PYQs and must not pretend to be — but they are also not a different exam. So `scripts/mh-ssc-10-text/` writes `question_kind='practice'` into the identical chapter rows and `/browse`'s existing PYQ/Practice toggle does the separating, with no new exam, no new taxonomy tree and no schema change. This is the first time the two kinds coexist on one chapter.
  - **The trap that would have silently split the corpus in two: the book's chapter spelling is not the DB's.** The textbook prints *"Historiography : Development in the West"* with a space before the colon; the DB row has none. Chapters auto-create on commit, so transcribing the book's spelling would have produced a second, near-identical chapter holding all the textbook rows while the PYQs stayed in the first — no error, no warning, just a chapter that reads right and a corpus cut in half. `config.ts` now carries `chapterName` as an explicit DB-spelling field with that reasoning inline.
  - **Taxonomy realigned to the book, on the user's instruction.** The PYQ-era subtopics had drifted from the printed section headings, so six were RENAMED in place (DB + `config.ts` + 11 committed PYQ data JSONs, byte-level so nothing else moved) rather than adding parallel names — one axis per chapter, shared by both kinds. Eight further sections the PYQs never touched are new (§3.5 Affiliated Professional Fields; Decentralisation of Democracy; Right to Information; and five Electoral-Process sections). **Political Science sections are UNNUMBERED in this book**, so those subtopics are the bold headings in printed order. One legacy subtopic, `Conduct of Elections` (8 PYQ rows), matches NO section of the book and was deliberately left alone and kept OUT of the config list — it needs per-question reclassification, not a rename, and that is logged rather than done.
  - **Same no-answer-key regime as the Class-9 humanities book, and it was verified rather than assumed** — a text-layer scan of all 112 pages confirms no answers section anywhere, so `config.ts` carries a block comment telling a later session not to "restore" a missing `answersPdf`. Compensating controls: answers authored strictly from the chapter's own prose, **all 12 MCQ keys blind-re-derived (0 mismatches)**, `audit-grounding.ts`, and `derived_model`/`derived_at` + a `pyq_note` clause on all 60 rows.
  - **The single sharpest result: the textbook settles a contradiction the board's own papers created.** The pilot's Q1(A)(1) asks who founded modern historiography — the same question whose 2026 and 2020 board papers keyed *Ranke* and *Voltaire* respectively (caught by the cross-paper option-set probe in July and resolved then in favour of Voltaire). The prescribed textbook says it outright — *"Thus, it is said that Voltaire was the founder of modern historiography"* — so the textbook row and the corrected PYQ row now agree, and the blind verifier reached Voltaire independently, writing the divergence from the standard Ranké attribution into the stored justification.
  - **The MCQ verification held on the one question I expected to bend.** *"The essence of democracy is …"* offers universal adult franchise as the answer general civics discourse would give — but the chapter's ONLY "central to democratic government" clause is *decentralisation of power*, its one use of "essence" attaches to the representative system (a predicate matching no printed option), and the four options map onto the chapter's four section headings with exactly one carrying the claim. I re-grepped the chapter myself rather than take the verifier's word. Answer stands on the book, with the outside-knowledge tension recorded in the solution so a student is not blindsided.
  - **Two textbook defects preserved, not smoothed.** Applied History's wrong-pair question contradicts the chapter's OWN *At a glance* list twice (it prints Ramman as a West Bengal dance where the list gives a Garhwal ritual theatre, and localises Ramlila to Uttar Pradesh where the list says North India) — the first contradiction is what makes that pair the intended answer, so both are named in the model answer. And The Electoral Process asserts both that the Delimitation Commission is a body in its own right (prose) and that it is a *committee of the Election Commission* (its own MCQ stem); the answer follows the prose and flags the discrepancy.
  - **Where the book cannot answer its own question, the answer says so.** Working of the Constitution asks *"Which decision of the Court has resulted in protection of honour and dignity of women?"* — the chapter names no case at all, lists only the subjects the Court has ruled on, and its own "Please understand" box sends students off to find the judgements themselves. The answer states that rather than supplying a plausible case name, which is the single easiest invention in the whole batch.
  - **The grounding probe was sharpened three times by its own false positives, each fix strictly noise-reducing and TDD'd** (`tests/mh-ssc-10-text-grounding.test.ts`, 12 cases): a capitalised run may CONTAIN a lowercase connector but never END on one (so "UNESCO for" was being tested as a unit and reported missing while UNESCO sat in the chapter); the all-stopword filter compared case-sensitively, so the lowercase "the" admitted by that same connector rule let pure glue like "So the" through; and a GFM pipe-table cell is a sentence boundary, without which one concept-map answer contributed five bogus "names" (*Casts*, *Prepares*, …). **6 → 1 candidate on the first chapter; 5 across all four, every one a run-join artefact whose components are individually in the chapter (verified one by one) — so ZERO genuine ungrounded claims across all 48 authored answers.** The probe also caught something real: it was MY OWN MCQ justifications, not an agent's, that imported outside knowledge twice — a claim that two books were "English works" (the chapter never states a language) and a line identifying three distractor names from general knowledge. Both rewritten. The rule applies to the reviewer too.
  - **Verified:** 48/48 committed with 0 dedup skips, 48 flipped PUBLIC, sections backfilled, `audit:text` · `audit:keys` · `board:lint` clean, full gate PASS. The one figure (a rotated nested-diamond concept map whose labels the text layer scatters unrecoverably) was cropped, re-cropped after inspection to drop a sliver of the neighbouring question, and confirmed to leak no answer — its inner cells are the blanks. **Owed, as on every chapter of this book: a human spot-check of the derived answers.**

- **2026-08-02 — MH State Board Class 9 GEOGRAPHY COMPLETE: all 12 chapters / 217 q PUBLIC (mh-sb-9 274 → 491), the hardest of the three books, and two durable figure fixes it forced (merges `1e7ffa9` + `d87465a`).** Piloted one chapter first at the user's request, then ran the other eleven.
  - **Analysis before code changed the shape twice.** Geography ships **no answers section** (like the humanities book) AND is almost entirely free-response: of 217 questions only **five** are four-option MCQs, all in Ch.7. Two more "tick the correct option" blocks (Ch.2, Ch.6) print only **THREE** choices. The user chose to ship those 11 as free-response with the choices in the stem — no invented fourth option, and no touching `normalizeMcqOptions`, which three shipped pipelines share. Net: the blind MCQ re-derivation that anchors History/PolSci contributes almost nothing here, so **the grounding audit and human review carry the weight** — stated plainly rather than implied by a green run. All 5 keys blind-re-derived, 0 mismatches; the verifier worked the time calculation itself (165°×4 min = 11 h eastward → Wed 9 p.m.) and cross-checked its method against the chapter's own 90°E example.
  - **THE FINDING that generalises: the grounding probe was systematically blind to this book.** Geography prints much of its content INSIDE maps and diagrams as **vector art the text layer cannot see** — fig 2.18's twelve plate names and every Kolhapur taluka came back "unsourced". I verified each against the rendered page (they are all real labels), then made `audit-grounding.ts` merge an optional **`data/<id>.figtext.json`** — labels transcribed off the figure and attributed to it — so a genuine invention still stands out instead of drowning in false positives. **Across all 12 chapters: ZERO genuine grounding violations.**
  - **Two figure-pipeline fixes, both forced by real failures.** (1) **A question's figure set can span a page break** — Precipitation's Q2/Q3 read figures (A),(B),(C) where (A) is on printed p48 and (B),(C) overleaf, so *no single bbox can show what the question asks about*; `attach-images.ts` now accepts a pre-made **`file`** in the manifest and those six rows share one stitched composite carrying all three with their captions. (2) **Near-full-page colour art blows the 1 MB storage cap** — the landform panorama rendered at 7.2 MB and the upload threw; ported the size-aware step-down-then-JPEG crop from `scripts/mh-ssc-10` (→ 458 KB at 3×), which the Urbanisation photo block and the Maharashtra tourist map would both have needed too. **18 figures, every crop verified individually** — two needed re-cropping after inspection (the IDL bbox first caught the MCQ block instead of the diagram; the tourism map first lost its northern edge).
  - **The agents' best work was refusing to answer.** Transport Ch.11 names **no** airport city, **no** port and **no** highway number, so 8 of its 11 rows carry a `[Note:]` naming the gap rather than a remembered list. Tourism's Q2 ten-row matching chain is unanswerable from the book — and rather than accept that at face value I sent the agent back to read fig 12.1; it reported at zoom that the map **prints no State names at all** and plots only 5 of the 30 names, so 28 of 30 cells stay "the chapter does not state this" with the student redirected to **an atlas, which is the chapter's own printed instruction**. Trade followed the book's EU=28 and its own organisation figures over current membership, caught that the SAARC statement is verbatim the book's **ASEAN** entry, and flagged that the exercise asks for "balance of payments" while the chapter defines only balance of trade.
  - **Ops:** the session limit killed four authoring agents mid-flight — these read page images and are far heavier than the humanities ones — so the rest ran in waves of three with each told to open **only** the page it needs. Nothing was lost: transcription had already completed and committed. Also re-confirmed that **Next's on-disk fetch cache (`.next/cache`) can serve a stale `/board` chapter list** across dev restarts AND production builds; a chapter appeared missing and 404'd until the cache was cleared — an ingest bug it was not.
  - **Honest gaps:** all 217 rows carry the derived-answer stamp and a human spot-check is still owed; Ch.2's answers rest on grounding discipline alone since it has no MCQs; and two Ch.2 textbook defects ship bracketed rather than smoothed (S-wave particles "move up and down **in the direction of energy transfer**", self-contradictory; Barren Island listed dormant while the book's own box calls it India's only active volcano).

- **2026-08-01 (second pass) — the Class-9 History + Political Science book COMPLETED: +11 chapters / +138 q (mh-sb-9 136 → 274), and a grounding audit built because there is no key to audit against.** Follows the 5-chapter pass below; the user's framing — *"i hope the answers are grounded in textbook content"* — set the whole shape of this pass.
  - **The hope was tested, not assumed.** Before adding a chapter, I built **`audit-grounding.ts`**: it extracts every 4-digit year and every proper noun from each stored answer and checks it against that chapter's own text layer. Run over the 58 already-shipped rows it surfaced 29 candidates; triaging every one found **zero fabrications** — the regionalism examples, the WWI powers, the LTTE assassination all trace to sentences in the book, and the riskiest-looking hit (an answer quoting "the History companion chapter" for "Pokharan **in Rajasthan**") is a *verbatim* sentence in the book, so the cross-chapter citation was honest. **The probe's first version was worthless and that is the transferable lesson**: its proper-noun regex spanned sentence boundaries, so `"Golden Temple. The army"` became one bogus token and real signal drowned in 67 hits. Splitting on sentence terminators first, and dropping sentence-initial capitals as grammatical rather than nominal, cut it to 29 — the same fix pattern as the [[stem-render-corruption-probes]] escaping traps. **It is deliberately one-sided and says so in its own header: it cannot see an invention phrased in ordinary lowercase, so a clean run is evidence, never proof.**
  - **Over the 11 new chapters (110 authored answers, 82 candidates) it found exactly ONE genuine violation** — an answer explaining that the right to information "is a statutory right under a 2005 Act", true but absent from the chapter. Removed; the answer stands on its chapter-based reasoning. Two more looked damning and were not: `SNDT Women's University` IS in the book (the probe missed it on a curly apostrophe) and `Director of BARC` is the book's own "Dr Raja Ramanna, Director of the Bhabha Atomic Research Centre", just abbreviated. **Both were resolved by grepping the source, not by trusting either the probe or the agent.**
  - **The authoring agents refused inventions unprompted, which is the real signal the brief worked.** The UN agent declined to name Dumbarton Oaks or Yalta because the chapter says only "a conference among the allied powers"; the Changing-Life-2 agent left the Olympic-medallist row at Karnam Malleshwari alone and omitted Doordarshan entirely; the Science-and-Technology agent refused to place the 1998 test at Pokharan because the chapter locates only the 1974 one, and caught itself crediting BSNL broadband to satellite technology, an unsupported causal link. One agent found its own placeholder row via the id diff the brief mandates — **a row that would otherwise have shipped with an empty answer**, which is exactly why "diff the id sets, don't trust your count" is in the brief.
  - **Three source defects preserved rather than corrected.** *Education Q1(2) prints only THREE options* (verified on the page image — no fourth hiding in a column break); fabricating one was out, so it ships as free-response with the three printed options in the stem plus an errata bracket. *The Economic Development five-year-plan chart* attributes "scientific research, health and family planning" to the Fourth Plan while the chapter's own prose gives that Plan entirely different aims — the textbook contradicting itself, flagged not reconciled. *International Problems Q2(2)* repeats India-and-Other-Countries Q2(2) verbatim; transcribed faithfully and left to `content_hash` dedup, which dropped it (139 transcribed → 138 committed) — the honest outcome, since forcing it in would need a fabricated distinguishing context.
  - **A judgement call the chapter forced, twice.** PS5 Q2(2) "Sino-Indian relations are friendly" → **False**, because the chapter says relations are *improving slowly* and reserves "friendly" for Sri Lanka, Maldives and Russia. PS4 Q2(2) "the status of all member nations is not equal" → **False** on the chapter's stated principle of equality, with an honest closing sentence that the veto exists — rather than silently picking a side. Same discipline as the Nehru/Vajpayee pair in the first pass.
  - **Scale + verification:** 11 transcription agents then 11 authoring agents + 1 blind verifier, all against a shared written contract. **All 28 new MCQ keys blind-re-derived, 0 mismatches** (40 of 40 across both passes). `audit:text` · `audit:keys` · `board:lint` clean; full gate PASS; all **17** `/board` chapter pages smoke-tested 200 with the route confirmed compiled, tables rendering as real `<table>`/`<th>` and all 5 figures reachable. Four figures cropped, each re-cropped after inspection (the concept map took three passes — first clipped the heading, second clipped the "Causes"/"Visible Effects" labels the answer depends on).
  - **Honest gaps unchanged:** every answer here rests on grounding discipline plus a blind MCQ pass, which is weaker than a book diff; all 196 rows carry the derived-answer stamp and a human spot-check is still owed.

- **2026-08-01 — MH State Board Class 9 gains History + Political Science: 5 chapters → 58 q PUBLIC (mh-sb-9 78 → 136), and the first State Board source that ships NO answer key at all.** The ask named three History and two Political Science chapters. Analysing the source before writing anything changed the shape of the job four times.
  - **The five chapters live in ONE book with TWO bank subjects.** `9th_Hist_SB.pdf` (108pp) is History ch.1-10 (printed pp 1-54) then Political Science ch.1-6 (pp 57-96), each with its own contents page. Two new subjects seeded under mh-sb-9, spelled exactly as under mh-ssc-10 so Class 9 → 10 stays consistent. Chapter names come from the book's **own contents pages**, not the pre-split `9th_Chapters/` folder names, which paraphrase ("The Progress of India's Foreign Policy" vs the printed "India's Foreign Policy") — and those splits also drop the odd opener page (PS Ch.1 starts at printed p57, its split PDF at p58), so rendering stays on the whole book at index = printed page + 9.
  - **THE FINDING: this textbook has no ANSWERS section anywhere** — verified by scanning the full text layer of all 108 pages. Both Maths books have one, and the end-of-book cross-check is a mandatory workflow gate that has caught real errors on both sides *every time it has run* (151 errata across 12 Class-12 chapters; on Class-9 Sets it caught a Venn-figure transcription error of ours). **That gate simply cannot run here**, so all 12 MCQ keys are derived and all 46 model answers authored with nothing to diff them against. Compensating controls: answers authored strictly from the chapter's own prose (for recall-shaped humanities questions the book states the answer nearly verbatim, which is exactly why the grounding rule is enforceable), a blind MCQ re-derivation, and honest provenance in the data. `config.ts` carries a block comment so a later session does not "restore" a missing `answersPdf`.
  - **`reviewFlag` turned out to persist NOTHING.** The plan said "reviewFlag on every row", copying mh-ssc-10 — but reading its `lib.ts` shows `reviewFlag` only pushes a build-time console message; there is no such column. The durable markers that DO exist are `derived_model`/`derived_at` (already used this way on 560 rows) plus `pyq_note`, so all 58 rows carry both, recording that answers are derived, that no book cross-check was possible, and that a human spot-check is owed. **Lesson: verify a flag actually reaches the database before claiming a row is flagged.**
  - **Extraction is TEXT-FIRST, the reverse of every prior State Board chapter.** The prose text layer is clean (1.5k-3.8k chars/page) and carries no mathematical notation, so the unicode-math-flattens-to-`�` problem that forces vision on the Maths books does not exist; vision was needed only for one figure and to settle table layout. New `dump-text.ts` — and its output path is the load-bearing detail: **`render.ts` `rmSync`s `out/<id>/` before rasterising**, so the first run's dumps were silently deleted (the known mh-ssc-10 trap, hit anyway). Fixed durably by writing `out/<id>.text.md` as a SIBLING of that directory, making the two scripts order-independent rather than documenting a required order.
  - **Projects + inline activity boxes deliberately NOT ingested** (~13 + ~15 across the five chapters): "collect pictures of the brave soldiers of Kargil war", "get information on Manipur from the internet" — open-ended prompts with no determinate answer, where any model answer would be invented rather than grounded. User-confirmed before the build.
  - **Verification held on both flagged judgement calls, and the standing probe produced a false positive.** The blind verifier agreed with all 12 committed keys (0 mismatches) and flagged two for a second eye; I adjudicated both against the printed text. *UN's main responsibility* → the chapter's only UN sentence is "maintain global peace and security", so "to avoid war" stands (the near-identical phrase elsewhere describes the League of Nations, which the exercise evidently drew on — same answer either way). *"important aspect of India's foreign policy"* → the book's near-verbatim rival "India's nuclear policy is an important part of India's foreign policy" points at the distractor, but that option reads nuclear **development** — a capability, not a policy aspect — so non-alignment stands. `audit:keys` then flagged that same row `SOLN≠KEY`: a **false positive**, its `concludedLetter` matching the "(d)" inside the caveat *explaining why (d) is wrong*. Key was correct; the caveat was reworded to name the option text rather than its letter, which keeps the pedagogy and permanently silences the probe. Both audits now clean.
  - **The subjective/objective split is inverted from a Maths chapter** — 46 subjective to 12 MCQ, and three chapters' worth of answers are prose. Two stems carry GFM pipe-tables (a wrong-pair table, a WWI/WWII comparison), verified rendering as real `<table>`/`<th>`/`<td>` on `/board` rather than raw pipes. One figure (the Foreign Policy concept map, five empty boxes round a centre) took two re-crops — the first sliced the question heading, the second cut the bottom boxes. `/board` outlines are a single `exercise` block per chapter: this book has **no solved-example bucket at all**, so unlike Maths there was nothing shippable off the book's own working.
  - **Honest gaps:** 58 rows is small (a humanities chapter is 4-7pp with a one-page exercise) and all 16 chapters of this book would total ~180; several subtopics hold 1-2 questions, which resolves only as the remaining 11 chapters land; and every answer here rests on grounding discipline plus a blind MCQ pass, which is genuinely weaker than a book diff — hence the derived-provenance stamp on all 58.

- **2026-08-01 — JEE Mains PHYSICS COMPLETE: 1,845 → 3,482 q PUBLIC across 88 papers, level with Maths; ~13 wrong source keys caught; JEE bank now 0 KaTeX-broken (merges `5042d67` · `71c741a` · `3e61121`).** The ask was "ingest all remaining JEE Physics PYQs", and the user's correction — *"all papers contain physics. you will have to look into actual papers or read math extrction codebase"* — reframed the job before any code was written.
  - **THE KEY DISCOVERY: Physics was ALREADY EXTRACTED.** Extraction is subject-agnostic and only the DB *commit* filtered by subject, so ~3,685 Physics rows already sat in `out/*.records.json` from the Maths runs. The job was classify + verify, not extract. `dump-maths.ts` → **`dump-subject.ts --subject=Physics`**. Reading the pipeline (as instructed) rather than reasoning from counts is what surfaced it.
  - **The BLIND lane repeatedly justified itself.** Papers were triaged SAFE (trustworthy numbering) vs BLIND (key untrustworthy → derive every answer, ignore the key). The decisive case: **2023-feb01's ENTIRE shift-2 key block is displaced by +2**, confirmed against 18 independent derivations — trusting it would have shipped ~13 wrong answers from one paper. Twelve further single keys were proven wrong, each re-derived before acceptance: torque `[ML²T⁻²]` gives 25% not 5%; two Carnot engines in series match one ("same", not "always less"); constant velocity makes the contact force `Mg`, not `Mg cosθ`; a plane EM wave's electric and magnetic energy densities are EQUAL so the ratio is 1, not 2; Young's modulus DECREASES with temperature; 7 mA less a 1.5 mA load leaves 5.5 mA in the Zener, not 10; `a sinθ = 2λ` gives a 6e-3 rad divergence where the key read 60; AM bandwidth is `2f_m` (the booklet wrote the right expression then evaluated `2ω_c`). In nearly every case the booklet's own worked solution contradicted its own printed letter.
  - **A whole paper was nearly lost.** A completeness audit diffing every `papers/*.json` against the DB found **2023-jan30 with ZERO Physics rows** — never dispatched, and the subject would have been declared finished with a sitting missing. It had to reconcile BOTH directions: the 11 legacy 2021 papers legitimately have DB rows but no paper-file classification. → [[completeness-reconcile-both-ways]].
  - **`scan-flip` published rows it had no business touching.** It matched on `source_file` alone, but a JEE file carries all three subjects, each ingested in its own pass — so flipping Physics for 2023-Jan29 also published **Maths Q67/Q70**, which the Maths pass had deliberately withheld for corrupted options. Both restored to PRIVATE; every recorded withhold across the exam was then audited (apr10 Q171/175, jan30 Q176, jul28 Q69, Paper9 Q84, p19 Q18 — all correctly PRIVATE, Jan29 the only breach). `scan-flip` now takes `--subject`, scoping both the scan and the UPDATE, and warns on an unscoped flip. → [[scope-writes-to-your-pass]].
  - **A build failure whose obvious diagnosis was wrong.** `next build` began failing on the `/questions` landing pages with "canceling statement due to statement timeout", a DIFFERENT handful each run. The natural read was a missing index — but **EXPLAIN showed the chapter query runs in 13 ms** on an existing index. The real cause is contention: Next prerenders 300+ of those pages concurrently and this session's ~1,600 new rows tipped the burst over Postgres' `statement_timeout`. An index would have fixed nothing. `queryQuestions` now retries ONLY a cancelled statement (SQLSTATE 57014, with a message fallback since PostgREST does not always pass the code), twice with a short backoff; failing-twice → clean.
  - **Structured repairs beat prose.** The BLIND solvers were correctly diagnosing dropped-digit stems but recording the repair as *prose*, which nothing can apply — a question would have shipped with a correct answer and a still-unanswerable stem. The prompt now demands a structured `stemFix`/`optionFix`, and the applier **refuses any fragment that does not match exactly once** rather than force-applying a near-miss (~300 repairs applied). `assemble-blind` also now merges into an existing paper file instead of clobbering it (every BLIND paper already carried a shipped Maths block) and treats a row whose options were absorbed into the stem as shippable when all four labels are supplied — `commit.ts` could already synthesize them, but the assembler was dropping such rows first.
  - **Render cleanup, bank-wide:** 351 escaped fill-in blanks (`\_\_\_\_` renders literally outside math), 54 empty math zones, 19 KaTeX-breaking zones (a body ending in a lone `\`; `\text{∮}` → `\oint`), and finally 8 Maths rows where pandoc split a set `\{…\}` across zones and orphaned the braces — reconstructed by hand, then **persisted into the paper files as overrides** so a later `resync` is a no-op rather than a silent revert (→ [[persist-fix-to-source-of-record]]). **KaTeX-broken: 47 → 0 of 7,436**; render-corruption 221 → 6.
  - **Judgement calls made AGAINST the agents, in both directions.** Rejected jan31 Q22 (its uniqueness argument only tried integer thicknesses; `K = t/(t−1)` is equally clean for t = 1.5, 4/3, 1.25) and Q92 (its own note conceded the opposite reading gives another option) — jan31 being the one paper with **no solution document at all**, so it ran under a stricter brief (double-derive, falsify distractors, per-row confidence, skip readily). Conversely I nearly rejected p24 Q29, whose *stated* justification was an aesthetic "drop-pattern" prior — until the booklet's own working showed `250 + 150 = 400`. → [[classify-findings-by-evidence]] sharpened: an agent's stated reasoning is not its evidence, in either direction.

- _Entries dated **2026-07-29 and earlier** are archived in [DECISIONS_HISTORY.md](DECISIONS_HISTORY.md) (batches 5–8 span 2026-06-01 → 2026-07-29). The Foundations (M1–M3) sub-section above + everything **2026-08-01 onward** stays here; archive older months to keep this active log to ~the last 2 weeks._

## Notes editorial workflow

The full step-by-step workflow for authoring a `/notes` chapter lives in **[NOTES_WORKFLOW.md](NOTES_WORKFLOW.md)** — **read it before building one.** It covers: Step 0 pedagogy-first structure (reshape the bank to the teaching arc; read HARD+MODERATE solutions; MHT-CET-Maths defaults) · Step 1 the `ConceptUnit` formula/reference discriminated union + the diagram decision rule + concept-content alignment · Step 1b author-for-clean-quiz-harvest · Step 2 the `NOTES_CHAPTERS` registry · Step 3 the chat tagging session (set-sibling co-location, 100% coverage) · Step 4 the gate chain (`notes:lint`/`notes:latex`/`notes:coverage`/`quiz:coverage`/`prepush`) · Step 5 ship + `npm run stats` + `notes:order` · the invariants to verify before commit.

## After a new upload

When a new question-batch lands, run the 6-step ritual in order. The upload pipeline is auto (taxonomy auto-creates, `content_hash` dedup, default PUBLIC visibility, live counts in `/browse` + dashboard + `/guide` TOP_11 + `/notes` drills). What's NOT auto and needs this ritual: **tag coverage, taxonomy fragmentation cleanup, and TS-static editorial copy**.

1. **Sanity-check the batch** (always) — confirm `inserted` vs PUBLIC/PRIVATE counts, PYQ metadata stamped, no missing years
2. **Taxonomy delta cleanup** (if ≥5 new subtopic auto-creates or a new chapter name) — Phase D delta inline; don't restart from scratch. Precedent: 2026-05-17 NDA Maths re-Phase-D (~770 new auto-creates closed in one session)
3. **Principle-tag delta survey** (if new questions in TOP_11 chapter footprints) — incremental per-new-chapter, not whole-bank
4. **Concept-tag delta session** (if new questions in `/notes`-covered subtopics) — also revisit dropped-gap ConceptUnits; bank growth can mature what was previously dropped
4b. **Notes-coverage check** (if new questions in a `/notes`-covered chapter) — `npm run notes:coverage <subjectRoute> <chapterSlug>` and triage the macro/technique channel. New uploads are how a *technique gap* drifts in (a fresh question using a method the notes don't teach); this is the routine catch for it. Add a concept for any real gap.
5. **Editorial copy refresh** (if counts move ≥5–10%) — `whyItMatters`, OVERVIEW constants, CHAPTER_TABLE, strategy %HARD, trends callouts. Mechanical TS edits
6. **Integrity check** (always) — `npm run notes:lint` + `npm run prepush` before commit

Steps 2–5 (incl. 4b) are conditional and can be deferred / queued across sessions. Full procedure + SQL probes + skip-conditions: [[post-upload-ritual]].

## Adding a new RLS-protected table

Follow the `questions`/`options` pattern (see `0002_rls.sql` + `0004_upload_staging.sql`):
1. Enable RLS.
2. Read policy: `org_id = private.current_user_org_id()`.
3. Write policy: same plus `private.current_user_is_admin()` if admin-curated.
4. Add a test in `tests/upload-rls.test.ts` (or new equivalent) proving a TEACHER cannot write and a member of another org cannot read.
5. Run `mcp__supabase__get_advisors` after applying the migration.

## Live infra

- **Supabase project:** `wunvtnqlzjrkvolslbnm` (https://wunvtnqlzjrkvolslbnm.supabase.co) — **plan: PRO.** Egress 250 GB included (2026-08-13: **1.73 GB used, 0 overage**, ~0.7%). Recorded here because a plan upgrade leaves NO trace in the repo — no migration, no config, no commit — so the only way a later session learns it is this line or the provider's usage page. The free-tier "5 GB / 11.48 GB" figures in the 2026-08-05 Decisions entry are history; treating them as live cost an hour on 2026-08-13. See [[project-docs-staleness]].
- **Production:** **https://www.pyqvault.com** (custom domain, live 2026-06-04; apex `pyqvault.com` 308-redirects to `www`) — auto-deploys from `main`. The old `question-bank-sage.vercel.app` still resolves (optionally set to redirect → `www.pyqvault.com` in Vercel → Domains). Canonical URL in code is `https://www.pyqvault.com` (the 4 `SITE_URL` consts: layout/sitemap/robots/GuideJsonLd). DNS at GoDaddy: `A @ → 216.198.79.1` + `CNAME www → <unique>.vercel-dns-NNN.com`. **Vercel plan is Hobby** — fine for the free launch, but move to **Pro before Razorpay goes live** (taking payments = commercial use).
- **Repo:** https://github.com/lwspune/question-bank (public; Vercel project is named `question-bank`)
- **Auth callback URLs allow-listed in Supabase Auth (Authentication → URL Configuration):** `https://www.pyqvault.com/api/auth/callback` + `https://pyqvault.com/api/auth/callback` + the legacy `question-bank-sage.vercel.app` + `localhost:3000`. Site URL = `https://www.pyqvault.com`. (Adding the new domain here is mandatory or OAuth/email sign-in breaks on it.)
